Company - Competitive advantage

❌ D477 questions
C-4150C-4256C-4360C-4457C-4530C-4640C-4742

Group judgement written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

Judgement. Bravida has a defensible position but not an economic moat.

What is defensible.

  • Density. A service franchise of about half of revenue.
  • Cycle resilience. Financial strength and a self-funded acquisition engine.
  • A widening capability in complex projects that only one Nordic rival matches.

What is missing. Every test of economic advantage fails or is unproven:

  • pricing power is absent;
  • purchasing power is weak;
  • scale is not shared;
  • financial outputs do not compound;
  • utility is non-inferior but not superior.

The 2026 to 2028 thesis rides on the widening complex-project advantage, which is also where value leaks to equipment makers and subcontractors.

Implication for the second set.

  • Advantages justify modelling resilient volume and share gains in complex projects.
  • They do not justify a margin above the 5.2% to 6.9% envelope, except through specific, evidenced levers: Finland, the Swedish savings, mix and drop-through.
  • A fair multiple for this profile is near the stock's ten-year median (about 14.6x forward earnings), not its upper quartile.
C-41

Which sustainable relative competitive advantages or moats does the company hold, how strong is each, and are they widening or narrowing?

C50

Question asked Which sustainable relative competitive advantages or moats does the company hold (customer-related; production-related; scale-based; regulatory or political; culture or knowledge), how strong is each, and are they widening or narrowing?

Judgement. Bravida's advantages are real but narrow. They are advantages of position (density, references, financial strength). They are not advantages of economics: the margin envelope has been 5.2% to 6.9% for ten years and the gross margin about 15%.

Customer-related: service lock-in. Moderate, stable.

  • Service is 48% of sales.
  • Service contracts run three to seven years, and warranties on complex systems lapse if a third party maintains them.
  • 91% of customers above SEK 5m renew; NPS was 58 against a target of 50.
  • Against that, an expert estimates about 30% churn of the service portfolio over five years.

Production-related.

  • Bid control: group-wide estimating and risk systems, with the chief executive signing off tenders above SEK 50m. Moderate. It was breached in Denmark (2023 to 2024) and Finland (2025), so it is not strong.
  • Complex-project capability: about 200MW of data centres since 2009, a special-projects office of about 70, and white-collar depth. The strongest advantage, and widening, as repeat awards and the region's largest order show. Only ACG matches it.

Scale-based.

  • Branch density: about 330 branches, competing on "density over absolute size" and local response time. Moderate, stable, and compounded by bolt-ons.
  • Purchasing scale: weak. It is "heavily exaggerated" (a Caverion strategist): there is no unified procurement system and only 50 to 60 central buyers.

Regulatory: none. Since Region Skåne, regulation is a cost.

Culture or knowledge: the Bravida Way.

  • It gives fixed costs 2 to 3 points lower than unified peers.
  • The same branch autonomy produced the compliance failure.

Net direction. Widening only in complex projects. Stable in service and density. Narrowing in market share (8.5% in 2025, from 9.0%). For the model, the advantages support volume and resilience, not a premium margin.

C-42

How large is the company's scale relative to competitors and other actors, and what does that scale confer?

C56

Question asked How large is the company's scale relative to competitors and other actors (revenue, headcount and so on), and what does that scale confer?

Judgement. Bravida is large for the Nordics and mid-sized for Europe.

Size against peers.

  • SEK 28.2bn of revenue, about 13,000 to 13,400 staff and about 330 branches.
  • About 2x Instalco (SEK 13.6bn); below ACG (about SEK 41bn); about a quarter of SPIE (EUR 10.4bn).

What scale confers.

  1. Local density, and so response time and technician utilisation.
  2. Balance-sheet strength. On mega-projects this earns a "risk-adjusted premium simply for being a financially stable supplier", and it lets Bravida carry milestone working capital.
  3. The ability to staff very large sites. Few Nordic competitors can field 40 to 60 site managers.
  4. A self-funded acquisition engine.

What it does not confer.

  • Purchasing power.
  • Operating-margin superiority: Instalco has historically run about 8% at half the scale.

For the model. Scale is a moat for winning complex contracts and surviving downturns. It is not a source of margin expansion.

C-43

How large is the realistic total addressable market relative to the company, and what rate of growth does it allow for over the mid term?

C60

Judgement. The addressable market is large relative to Bravida's 8.5% share, but slow-growing. Market growth alone allows about 4% to 5% nominal organic growth through the cycle; 12% to 20% EPS growth needs margin and acquisitions on top.

Size. The Nordic multi-technical market is about SEK 180 to 209bn, with Sweden about SEK 130bn.

Growth.

  • About 4.5% a year historically, against regional GDP growth of 2.2% to 2.5%.
  • About 8% a year from 2019 to 2024, fuelled by inflation.
  • About 3% a year in real terms expected to 2029.

Faster-growing pools.

  • Nordic data centres: capacity growth well above market, though Bravida's addressable slice is the installation package.
  • Defence and transport infrastructure.
  • EPBD-driven renovation of the worst 16% of non-residential stock by 2030.

Mid-term growth this allows.

  • Organic 4% to 6% a year in 2026 to 2028 with the data-centre layer, or 3% to 5% without it.
  • Acquisitions add 1% to 3% at the current pace, or 5% to 7% at the target pace.
  • Total revenue growth of 6% to 9% a year is plausible. The 7.6% a year in consensus for FY2025 to FY2028 sits inside that range, at the upper half, because of the contracted data-centre work.
C-44

How high is the company's surplus customer utility against competing or alternative offerings, and is it leading, or at least demonstrably not inferior?

C57

Question asked How high is the company's surplus customer utility against competing or alternative offerings, and is it leading, or at least demonstrably not inferior? (Hard to prove, easier to disprove; the aim is to disprove inferiority at Early-Stage and Deep Dive, for example Sophos, Bytes.)

Judgement. Bravida is demonstrably not inferior. Leading utility is not shown.

The evidence against inferiority.

  • 94% to 98% of customers are recurring.
  • 91% renewal among customers above SEK 5m.
  • NPS of 58 against a target of 50 (2024), from over 1,100 client meetings a year.
  • Named customers praise it: Skanska, EcoDataCenter, Sykehusbygg, Baneservice.
  • It has repeat data-centre business, including EcoDataCenter's eighth project, and won the region's largest data-centre order.

Where it is not shown to lead.

  • In 2025 it lost share against Instalco in Finland (-14% against +30%).
  • It trails ACG and Instalco on margin, which would be the economic trace of superior utility.

For the model. Customer utility is good enough to sustain retention and win complex work. It does not support price rises above cost.

C-45

To what extent does the company have, and use, demonstrable pricing power with minimal erosion of its relative surplus customer utility, shown by evidence of application rather than conjecture?

❌ D30

Judgement. Bravida does not have demonstrable pricing power in its core, and there is no evidence of it applying real price rises.

What the record shows.

  • The ten-year margin envelope is flat: 6.2% in 2015, 5.9% in 2025.
  • Gross margin has stayed at 14.2% to 15.2%.
  • Index clauses lag spot costs, and Swedish contracts are not automatically indexed.
  • Management concedes price is the industry's lever for growth: "they just lower the prices".
  • Cost pass-through takes 9 to 12 months, and margins compressed in the 2022 to 2024 inflation.

Where pricing power is latent. In complex projects: a stability premium, and "execution certainty matters more than lowest cost". It is not yet visible in group numbers.

The coming test. Swedish minimum wages +8% in 2027.

For the model. Price contributes zero real margin. Cost inflation passes through with a lag that creates a temporary squeeze when costs accelerate.

C-46

To what extent does the company share its scale economies with customers, stated or de facto, in its messaging, strategy, culture or values?

❌ D40

Judgement. Bravida does not share scale economies with customers.

  • It does not do so in stated strategy or de facto.
  • Its "margin over volume" ethos is the opposite: it declines low-margin work rather than undercutting.
  • It extracts a premium for stability on complex work, and its purchasing scale is too weak to share.

What this means for the case.

  • There is no scale-sharing flywheel of the Costco or Ryanair kind: lower price, more volume, lower cost.
  • So no mechanism exists by which scale mechanically widens the advantage.
  • The case does not depend on one, but it removes a source of durable outperformance.
C-47

To what extent do gravitational or mechanical forces, rather than heroic company dynamism, improve the company's competitive advantage and its financial outputs against peers with each period that passes?

❌ D42

Judgement. Mostly heroic, partly gravitational.

The gravitational elements.

  • Density compounds with each bolt-on.
  • Complex-project references compound with each delivered data centre.
  • The service base compounds through installation-to-service conversion.

The financial outputs do not compound.

  • Margin was 6.2% in 2015 and 5.9% in 2025.
  • ROE fell from 17.4% (2021) to 13.8% (2025).
  • Share fell in 2025.
  • Each recent improvement needed a heroic intervention:
  • the chief executive as interim head of Denmark;
  • the dismissal in Division South;
  • the Swedish merger;
  • the Finnish branch restructuring.

For the model. Do not assume structural margin drift upward. Assume margin holds only as long as management keeps intervening. The decentralised model reverts toward the industry's tail behaviour in regions where leadership is weak (Finland now, Denmark in 2023).

Back to contents

Company - Economic strength

C5211 questions
C-4966C-5045C-5155C-5245C-5342C-5455C-7545C-7666C-7745C-7857C-7970

Group judgement written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

Judgement. Bravida's economics are average for a listed company and decent for an installer.

What the economics are.

  • A 6% EBITA margin on a 15% gross margin.
  • Low capital intensity.
  • Historically excellent but now lumpy cash conversion.
  • Respectable, falling returns: ROE 13.8%.
  • Low financial leverage.
  • Little fixed-cost operating leverage.

What drives EPS.

  • EPS has historically grown with revenue: 5.5% a year since 2019, flat FY2023 to FY2025.
  • The 2026 to 2027 acceleration is real and well-supported on revenue: a contracted backlog and easy comparisons.
  • The margin step consensus requires is the weak point.

The margin step consensus requires.

  • 6.3% clean in FY2026 to 7.0% in FY2027 and 7.1% in FY2028, above anything sustained in ten years.
  • It needs about 12% drop-through on SEK 5 to 7bn of extra revenue, of which about 40% is 80%-subcontracted data-centre work.
  • It must absorb Swedish wage inflation and copper.
  • It has to come from a few heroic regional fixes (Finland, Sweden, Norway). Gravitational forces will not supply it.

Implications for the second set.

  1. Build revenue from the backlog and data-centre phasing with confidence.
  2. Build margin bottom-up by segment, with a base case of about 6.6% to 6.9% by FY2027 to FY2028. That puts EPS about 4% to 8% below consensus in FY2027 to FY2028.
  3. Model cash conversion as front-loaded strong (advances plus Stavanger) and back-loaded weak (2028 to 2029).
  4. Model below-EBIT items explicitly: interest about SEK 100 to 160m, tax about 21%, share count about -0.5% a year.
  5. The plausible cone for FY2027 EPS is about SEK 7.2 to 9.4. Consensus SEK 8.78 sits near its top.
C-49

What earnings-per-share growth is the company generating, with what revenue growth, and how durable is it?

C66

Judgement. Near-term EPS growth is high and well-supported. Through-cycle EPS growth is moderate and revenue-led.

The record: EPS has grown with revenue, not with margin.

  • Adjusted EPS rose from SEK 1.43 (2015) to SEK 6.03 (2025), about 15% a year. Much of that came early, from post-IPO deleveraging and acquisitions.
  • Since FY2019, reported EPS grew 5.5% a year (SEK 4.36 to 6.02), exactly matching revenue (5.5% a year, SEK 20.4bn to 28.2bn).
  • Over FY2023 to FY2025 it was flat: brokers' basis SEK 6.05, 5.17, 5.88. Revenue grew 2.4% a year from FY2022 to FY2025.

Now: generation has inflected.

  • H1 2026 adjusted EBITA rose 16% on revenue +5.9%.
  • Q2 2026 adjusted EBITA rose 20% on +9.4%.

Consensus.

  • EPS of SEK 7.20, 8.78 and 9.45: +22%, +22%, +8%, or 17% a year for FY2025 to FY2028.
  • On a clean basis, about SEK 6.7 to 6.9 in FY2026, with FY2027 growth of about 25% to 30%.

Durability by horizon.

  • FY2026 to FY2027: high visibility for revenue, moderate for margin.
  • The backlog is 1.34x last-twelve-month installation sales, the data-centre ramp is contracted, and the Swedish savings and comparisons are easy.
  • The margin step (6.3% to 7.0% in consensus) is the less certain part (see C-53 and C-75).
  • FY2028: lower. Comparisons harden, the data-centre peak passes, Swedish wages reset and rates are higher.
  • Beyond FY2028: underlying EPS growth of about 6% to 9% a year, unless the data-centre book refills:
  • organic 3% to 5%;
  • acquisitions 1% to 3%;
  • margin of 0 to 10bp a year;
  • buybacks about 0.5%.

For the model. A clean EPS path of roughly SEK 6.7 to 6.9 (FY2026), 8.0 to 8.6 (FY2027) and 8.6 to 9.3 (FY2028), with FY2029 flat to down if no new data-centre award. Consensus sits at the top of that range for FY2027.

C-50

How much immediate scope is there to deploy capital or operating spend at a high return on new invested capital for three to five years, and what does that mean for mid- and long-term potential and the fair multiple?

❌ D45

Judgement. The scope is limited in size, though returns are adequate.

Capex and working capital.

  • Capex is below 1% of sales.
  • Working capital for data-centre milestones (perhaps SEK 0.3 to 0.6bn at peak) earns its return through the contracts.

Acquisitions.

  • Acquisitions are the only material reinvestment avenue.
  • At 5 to 6x EBITA before synergies they earn about 13% to 16% post-tax, or about 20%+ if synergies take the effective multiple to 3.5x.
  • They are deployable at roughly SEK 0.5 to 1.5bn a year (2% to 5% of sales) given target quality and private-equity competition. That is nowhere near the framework's 20% of sales.
  • 2025 deployed SEK 369m (FactSet), about 1.3% of sales.

What it means.

  • Mid- and long-term EPS growth is capped at the 6% to 9% underlying range above.
  • Excess cash will go to dividends and buybacks, not compounding.
  • A business growing about 7% with mid-teens returns on incremental capital and limited reinvestment merits a forward P/E near its ten-year median of about 14.6x (FactSet bands: 35th percentile 13.4x, 75th 16.9x).
  • The current 16.0x to 16.5x is above that, at the 95th percentile of two years.
C-51

How high are the returns on capital: post-tax return on new invested capital, on capital employed and on tangible capital?

C55

Judgement. Returns are respectable rather than high. They have fallen since 2021 and recover only if consensus margins are delivered.

ROE.

  • 13.8% in 2025, down from a 17.4% peak in 2021.
  • On consensus FY2027 net income of about SEK 1.79bn against equity of about SEK 10.3bn, ROE would return to about 17%.

Post-tax return on capital employed (not disclosed; estimated).

  • About 11% to 13% on equity plus net financial debt excluding leases (EBITA SEK 1.67bn at 21% tax over about SEK 10.5bn).
  • About 10% including leases.

Post-tax return on new capital (acquisitions at 5 to 6x EBITA): about 13% to 16% before synergies.

Return on tangible capital.

  • Very high, because net working capital is near zero or negative on the company's basis and capex is below 1% of sales.
  • It is not quantified in the evidence: the goodwill balance from 166 acquisitions dominates the equity.

For the model.

  • Returns are not a constraint on growth.
  • They are not high enough to justify a premium multiple on their own.
  • The ROE recovery to about 17% is itself a consensus-margin bet.
C-52

How high is the conversion of net income into free cash flow, and how do net working capital and similar items behave?

❌ D45

Judgement. Historically excellent, currently weak, and about to become lumpier.

History and now.

  • The company's cash conversion averaged 98% to 99% over 2015 to 2025 and peaked at 112% in mid-2024.
  • It fell to 79% for FY2025 and 77% for the last twelve months at Q1 and Q2 2026, against a target above 100%.
  • On FactSet's FCF over net income (which appears to deduct lease repayments), it was 43% in FY2025 and 107% in FY2024.

What actually drove the dip: unwinding customer advances, not receivables.

  • The FactSet working capital measure (receivables plus inventories less payables) was lower at Q2 2026 (SEK 6,536m) than at Q2 2025 (SEK 6,893m).
  • The CFO attributes the dip to a large contract "that had previously provided large cash advances" moving into execution.
  • On top of that, about SEK 1.2bn of disputed receivables has been trapped: Stavanger now won, and SEK 800m in Danish arbitration until 2028.

The forward pattern this implies. A timing pattern the model should build explicitly.

  • New data-centre contracts with milestone and prepayment terms bring cash in early (H2 2026 to 2027) and consume it later (2028 to 2029).
  • Add Stavanger (about SEK 465m), and FY2026 to FY2027 conversion could look strong (90% to 110%) and FY2028 to FY2029 weak.
  • This is the Risks report's "cash flow timing illusion" pattern.

Consensus.

  • FY2026 operating cash flow of SEK 2.51bn against SEK 1.45bn in FY2025 (+73%).
  • FCF of SEK 2.0 to 2.4bn a year, 1.2 to 1.3x net income.
  • That requires both Stavanger and advances.

Seasonality. Q3 operating cash flow is typically negative (Q3 2025: -SEK 111m). Q4 carries the year (Q4 2025: +SEK 1,161m).

C-53

How high are gross and operating profit margins, and how are they trending?

❌ D42

Judgement. Margins are low in absolute terms and improving modestly.

Levels.

  • Gross margin 14.2% to 15.2% (FY2019 to FY2025; 15.2% in FY2025).
  • Adjusted EBITA margin 5.2% (FY2024 trough), 5.9% to 6.0% (FY2025), 6.2% for the last twelve months to Q2 2026 (SEK 1,801m on SEK 29,020m).
  • The ten-year envelope is 5.2% to 6.9%; FactSet's adjusted EBIT history peaks at 6.5% in FY2021.
  • The earnings review says the margin was flat on a twelve-month basis. Its own quarterly table shows the last twelve months up about 0.2pp, so read it as modestly rising.

By quarter.

  • Q2 2026: 5.9% against 5.4% in Q2 2025.
  • Q1 2026: 4.9% adjusted against 4.5%.
  • Q4 is seasonally the highest (8.1% to 8.4%) and Q1 the lowest.

By segment.

  • Denmark 5.0% to 5.1% (from 0.1% to 1%).
  • Norway about 5.1%, protected by a 61% to 62% service mix.
  • Sweden 5.7% in Q2 (4.5% in Q1 including restructuring), against a historical 6.2% to 6.8%.
  • Finland 1.3% (Q1) and 3.2% (Q2).

Against peers. Instalco 7.1% (Q2 2026) and 8.5% (2025, as stated); ACG 7.9%; SPIE 7.6%.

Consensus margins.

  • 6.6%, 7.0% and 7.1% for FY2026 to FY2028.
  • FY2026 is about 6.3% clean.
  • So consensus has the margin at the top of, and then above, the ten-year envelope by FY2027.

For the model. The trend is up. The gap to peers says a 7% margin is achievable in principle, but Bravida has never sustained it. Treat 6.5% to 6.8% as the base case for FY2027 to FY2028, with 7%+ as the upside.

C-54

How clean is the earnings base: how large is the gap between adjusted and reported earnings; do one-offs, capitalised costs or other items flatter it; and does the tax rate or any other non-obvious leakage erode earnings per share?

C55

Judgement. The earnings base is moderately clean and honestly bridged by the company. FY2026 is the year to adjust carefully.

The adjusted-to-reported gaps.

  • FY2025: SEK 5.88 brokers' basis against SEK 6.02 reported.
  • Q2 2026: reported EBITA SEK 570m against adjusted SEK 452m (ABEKA gain +158, restructuring -40).
  • FY2026 reported will include about +SEK 158m of gain and about -SEK 70 to 80m of restructuring.

Items that flatter or threaten the base.

  1. Percentage-of-completion accounting on large projects. Losses are recognised late and suddenly; the precedents are Denmark, Finland and peers.
  2. Credit losses: Northvolt (about SEK 100m; a SEK 186m provision in 2025 is also cited).
  3. Goodwill is not amortised, so acquisition costs never hit EPS unless impaired.

There is no capitalised-cost issue. Capex is below 1% of sales.

Leakage.

  • The tax rate is 20.8% to 22%, near the Swedish statutory 20.6%; the ABEKA gain is probably tax-exempt.
  • Net interest of SEK 181m in FY2025 includes leases, and 100% of debt is floating.
  • Translation is unhedged: -2% of sales in 2025.
  • Share dilution is small: the incentive C-shares, 178,138 converted in May 2026, are offset by buybacks.

For the model. Use clean FY2026 EPS of about SEK 6.7 to 6.9 as the base for growth. Model net interest of about SEK 130 to 160m for FY2026 and SEK 100 to 140m for FY2027, rather than the roughly SEK 60m implied by consensus pre-tax profit.

C-75

How material is the fixed-cost operating leverage: how much does a given change in revenue move operating profit, and how wide a range of outcomes does that create?

❌ D45

Judgement. Fixed-cost operating leverage is low. The swing factor in operating profit is project gross margin, not volume.

Why it is low.

  • Production costs are about 84% of revenue and largely variable: labour, subcontractors, materials.
  • SG&A is about 9.3% of sales, and it has grown faster than revenue (8.6% of sales in FY2021).
  • The history confirms it: FY2022 revenue +20% gave a flat margin (6.4%); FY2025 revenue -4.9% gave a higher margin (+0.6pp); FY2024 revenue +0.8% gave EBIT -8% on Danish write-downs.

Observed drop-through.

  • Q2 2026: about 11% (adjusted EBITA +SEK 74m on revenue +SEK 655m).
  • H1 2026: about 14%.
  • So the incremental margin runs at about 1.5 to 2x the average margin, mainly from overhead absorption and the Swedish savings.

Sensitivities.

  • 1% of revenue is about SEK 17 to 34m of EBITA, or 0.8% to 1.6% of EPS.
  • 0.5pp of EBITA margin is about SEK 150 to 170m, or 7% to 8% of EPS. Margin matters about five times more than volume.

What consensus requires.

  • FY2027 needs about 12% drop-through on SEK 5.7bn of extra revenue against FY2025, which is consistent with Q2 2026.
  • But that holds only if the data-centre revenue, about 40% of the increment and 80% subcontracted, earns at least the group margin.
  • A build with data centres at a 6% margin and core drop-through of 10% gives about SEK 2.26bn of FY2027 EBITA (6.7%), about 5% below consensus SEK 2.37bn, or about SEK 0.4 of EPS.

The range of outcomes for FY2027.

  • Revenue SEK 32.5 to 35bn and margin 6.2% to 7.2% give EBITA of SEK 2.0 to 2.5bn and EPS of about SEK 7.2 to 9.4.
  • That is -18% to +7% around consensus SEK 8.78. Consensus sits near the top of the cone, and the skew is to the downside.
C-76

How material is the financial leverage or gearing: how much does it amplify earnings per share and risk, and how comfortable is it?

C66

Judgement. Financial leverage is low, comfortable and barely amplifies EPS.

Levels.

  • Net debt to EBITDA is 1.3x on the company's basis (Q2 2026), 1.68x on FactSet's including leases, and about 0.6 to 0.7x excluding IFRS 16.
  • The policy ceiling is below 2.5x.
  • SEK 2.5bn of revolving facilities is undrawn to 2028.
  • Net interest of SEK 181m (FY2025) against EBITA of SEK 1.67bn means about 9x cover.

What amplifies risk.

  1. 100% floating-rate debt. +100bp is about SEK 25 to 30m pre-tax, or about 1.2% of EPS.
  2. All financing is in SEK against NOK, DKK and EUR earnings. A 30% fall in both NOK and DKK would cut EBITDA by about SEK 222m and raise the ratio mechanically.
  3. Seasonal intra-year working-capital peaks in Q2 to Q3.

Peers for comparison. Instalco is at 2.6x and Fasadgruppen at 3.3x to 4.2x. Bravida's balance sheet is a relative strength and the funding source for buybacks and data-centre working capital.

For the model. Gearing will stay at 1.0 to 1.5x on the company's basis through 2028 on the base case, even with SEK 200 to 400m a year of buybacks.

C-77

How much of each major part of the cost base is cuttable or reducible?

❌ D45

Judgement. Little is cuttable without damaging the model.

Production costs (about 84% of revenue).

  • These are variable rather than cuttable: they flex with volume.
  • Headcount fell 4% when volume fell, which shows the flexibility. That is protection, not saving.

SG&A (about 9.3% of revenue, about SEK 2.6bn).

  • The Swedish merger takes out about SEK 65m, 2.5% of SG&A.
  • Similar consolidation in Finland and Norway might yield SEK 20 to 40m.
  • Digital-modernisation spend of SEK 80 to 100m a year is "past its peak" and could fall by perhaps SEK 20 to 40m.

Procurement (materials).

  • A unified purchasing system does not exist.
  • Peers claim 150bp margin uplifts from procurement programmes (Morgan Stanley), but an industry expert doubts Bravida's procurement can capture them.
  • Perhaps 0.2 to 0.5pp of gross margin over three years if built. This is a hypothesis.

Large cuts would undermine the branch-owner model, which is the source of the 2 to 3 points lower fixed cost. Branch closures and headcount cuts also risk losing skilled staff before the recovery (the Risks report's failure mode 7).

Total plausible cost reduction. About SEK 100 to 150m by FY2028 (0.3 to 0.4pp of margin), most of it already in consensus.

C-78

Beyond cutting costs, how much plausible scope is there to improve the financial outputs over the mid term; do peer precedents show it is achievable; would it come from a few big areas or many small ones; and is it congruent with the stated strategy?

C57

Judgement. There is plausible scope for about 0.6 to 1.0pp of margin over three to four years, from a few big areas. It is congruent with strategy, and peers show the level is achievable.

Where it would come from.

  1. Finland: from 1% to 3% toward 5%, on SEK 2.6 to 3.4bn, worth SEK 50 to 90m.
  2. Sweden: 5.7% toward the historical 6.5% to 6.8%, through savings and recovering volume, worth SEK 80 to 150m.
  3. Norway: 5.1% toward 6%, worth about SEK 50 to 60m.
  4. Mix: service growth and complex-project margins, of uncertain size.

Peer precedents.

  • Instalco has historically run about 8%, now 7.1%, on a 57% cost-plus contract mix.
  • ACG is at 7.9% after integration synergies.
  • SPIE rose from 6.7% (2023) to 7.6% (2025) on mix and selectivity.

Congruence. It fits the stated lever: "apply the Bravida Way to lagging geographies", proven in Denmark.

The constraint. Bravida has done this once at a time, region by region, never across the group at once. Group margin has never held above 6.9%.

For the model.

  • About 0.7pp is reachable by FY2028 in the base case: 6.0% to 6.2% today rising to 6.7% to 6.9%.
  • 7%+ is the upside case.
  • Consensus's 7.1% by FY2028 assumes the full scope is delivered while 2027 cost headwinds are absorbed.
C-79

How narrow is the business perimeter, how is it changing, and what does that mean for predictability?

C70

Question asked How narrow is the business perimeter (number of countries and business lines), how is it changing, and what does that mean for predictability?

Judgement. The perimeter is narrow, and narrowing slightly, which helps predictability.

  • Four countries: Sweden 47% to 48%, Denmark 25% to 26%, Norway 19%, Finland 8%.
  • Two activities: installation 52%, service 48%.
  • About 330 branches running one operating model.
  • ABEKA's sale removed the one non-core activity.

The change that reduces predictability is within the perimeter, not across it. Large data-centre projects add a lumpy, milestone-driven revenue and cash stream that the branch model does not produce.

For the model.

  • Model four countries times two activities, plus a separate data-centre project line in Norway, Finland and Sweden with its own phasing, margin and working-capital assumptions.
  • That gives the model good transparency. The company discloses organic growth, mix and margin by country every quarter.

Company - Management

C5511 questions
C-5548C-5657C-5762C-57b60C-5848C-5945C-6050C-6164C-6236C-6354C-6452

Group judgement written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

Judgement. Bravida's management is an above-median, action-proven operating team under a long-tenured chief executive.

  • It is good at regional turnarounds.
  • It is cautious in communication and conventional in capital allocation.
  • It is short of outlier standards in margin, culture and controls.

The record that matters most for the model.

  1. It reliably delivers specific, bounded fixes: Denmark, Sweden, Norway historically. The Finnish margin recovery and the SEK 65m Swedish saving should be modelled as probable.
  2. It has never delivered the group margin target. The consensus margin path above 7% should be modelled as unproven.
  3. It held up better than peers in the downturn, which supports the resilience of the base.
  4. It now carries a new risk class (principal-contractor data-centre work) with controls that are new and untested.

Governance. Fair to shareholders. EBITA-weighted incentives, thin internal audit and low director ownership leave the compliance tail in place.

Communication. Talks volume expectations down. That favours positive volume surprises and offers no anchor on margin.

C-55

How big is the company's ambition or vision, particularly in relation to peers?

❌ D48

Question asked How big is the company's ambition or vision (a force: growth as a priority, outcompeting, innovating, adapting, pushing), particularly in relation to peers?

Judgement. Bravida's ambition is real but modest in scale and stated quietly. It is smaller than its peers', and that is consistent with a disciplined operator rather than an outcompeting force.

What has been stated.

  • "My aim is for Bravida to emerge from the recession a stronger company" (the chief executive, January 2026).
  • To become "a central partner in regional AI infrastructure growth".
  • A shift "from defence to growth" as markets allow.

The targets have not moved. Sales growth above 5% and an EBITA margin above 7% are unchanged, undated, and the margin target has been missed for a decade.

Peers aim higher.

  • SPIE raised its 2028 margin target to 8.0% and projects EBITA above EUR 1bn and cumulative FCF above EUR 2bn.
  • Instalco's founder has a "margin, margin, margin" mandate to 8%, plus a German platform (Fabri) in a market five times the Nordic size.
  • ACG built a separate data-centre business area.

For the model. Do not expect management to push growth beyond what demand offers. Do not expect a new, higher target set that could re-anchor consensus. The upside surprise, if any, will come from contract wins, not from ambition.

C-56

How great and smart is the strategy, with right and bold strategic priorities?

C57

Question asked How great and smart is the strategy, with right and bold strategic priorities (an enabler): widening the moat over the mid term, growth as a priority, and a dynamic strategy that can capitalise on a plausible range of environments?

Judgement. The strategy is sound, congruent and cycle-tested. It is not great or bold: it widens the moat only at the top of the market.

What is right about it.

  • Margin over volume protected the base in the downturn. Margin rose from 5.2% to 6.0% while revenue fell 5%.
  • Density plus bolt-ons is the correct way to build a service franchise in a fragmented market.
  • Targeting complex, capacity-constrained projects (data centres, defence, infrastructure) aims at the part of the market where pricing is rational.
  • Choosing co-locators rather than standardised hyperscaler packages matches what the sector research finds earns the best pricing.

Where it is less smart.

  1. Selectivity cost share. 0.5pp of Nordic share was lost in 2025, and Finland lost ground to Instalco.
  2. The new principal-contractor role moves risk onto the balance sheet without visible new central controls beyond the special-projects office and chief-executive sign-off.
  3. No procurement strategy addresses the clearest economic weakness. There is no unified system and the team is 50 to 60 people.
  4. ABEKA was sold at a higher margin than the group's.

Dynamic across environments? Yes. The strategy flexes between defending margin in downturns and taking complex projects in upswings.

C-57

How great is the chief executive, judged by actions rather than conjecture?

C62

Question asked How great is the chief executive, judged by actions rather than conjecture (an enabler, not an ossifier)?

Judgement. Mattias Johansson is an above-median operator, proven by action under stress. He is an enabler, not an ossifier. He is not an outlier: the standards and the margin record fall short.

Actions.

  • In Norway (2013 to 2014) he turned around a division at a 2.9% margin and lifted sales from SEK 2,375m to SEK 2,818m.
  • In Denmark he took the interim role personally (late 2023), hired Alsø, and saw the margin go from 1% to 5%.
  • He dismissed the Head of Division South the day the over-billing was confirmed.
  • He merged Sweden into one organisation.
  • He pivoted to data centres and won the largest order in company history.
  • He sued Örnsköldsvik after police cleared Bravida.

Record since 2015.

  • Revenue doubled from SEK 14.2bn to 28.2bn.
  • Adjusted EPS rose from SEK 1.43 to 6.03.
  • Cash conversion averaged 99%.
  • EPS rose 16% in FY2025 despite a construction recession.

Against him.

  • The Region Skåne fraud happened on his watch, and there was no internal audit function.
  • The 7% margin target has never been met.
  • All three FY2025 targets were missed.
  • Culture metrics are weak.
  • Pay of SEK 17.27m is 2.96x Instalco's chief executive's.
  • He has made no discretionary share purchases; his 758,773 shares came through incentive plans. They are worth about SEK 113m, which is material personal alignment.

For the model.

  • He makes regional recoveries credible: Finland and Sweden.
  • He makes "above 7%" less credible than consensus assumes.
  • Key-person risk is high: an 11-year tenure, no public external successor; Alsén is the named internal candidate.
C-57b

How high a priority is hiring well for the chief executive, and how great is the leadership team the chief executive has built?

C60

Question asked How high a priority is hiring well for the chief executive, and how great is the leadership team the chief executive has built (an enabler)?

Judgement. Hiring is evidently a priority, and the hires have delivered where tested.

  • Alsø (from ISS Denmark) delivered Denmark from 0.1% to 5.1%.
  • Täuber turned around Division South and now runs all of Sweden.
  • Vranjes (Telia, Ericsson) brought balance-sheet optimisation; she has overseen the buybacks.
  • Alsén (ex-BCG partner) is a credible successor.
  • A. Johansson was promoted internally to Head of M&A, which preserves continuity.
  • Pipeline: an internal recruitment agency and a 30-person Nordic trainee programme support it.

Gaps.

  • Finland's leadership has not yet produced a turnaround.
  • The CFO and the Head of M&A are untested at the new project scale and acquisition pace.
  • The board is independent, meets 13 times a year with 97% attendance, and is 57% female. But it lacks senior installation-industry experience, and directors other than the chief executive own only 70,745 shares.
C-58

How well resourced and invested is the whole business?

❌ D48

Question asked How well resourced and invested is the whole business (a buffer; an ant colony: built yesterday for resilience)?

Judgement. The business is adequately resourced for its traditional model, under-resourced in control, and thin on central capacity for its new project class.

Invested where it counts for delivery.

  • 5D BIM.
  • Building automation (about SEK 1bn of sales).
  • A special-projects office of about 70.
  • Digital-modernisation spend of SEK 80 to 100m a year, now past its peak.
  • Apprenticeships and the trainee intake.

Under-invested.

  • No dedicated internal audit function across about 330 branches.
  • Only 50 to 60 central procurement staff.
  • Supplier audits: only one supplier was audited in 2025.
  • Headcount was cut 4%. That creates capacity risk as volume and data-centre work ramp together, and it explains the 80% reliance on subcontractors.

Financially well buffered. Gearing of 1.3x, SEK 2.5bn of undrawn facilities and capex below 1% of sales.

For the model. The buffer is financial, not operational. Watch for capacity strain in the traditional book in 2027: a tight labour market, wage resets, and own staff diverted to data-centre sites.

C-59

How holistic and congruent is the culture, with alignment across the total system?

❌ D45

Question asked How holistic and congruent is the culture, with alignment across the total system (as at Lifco, Avanza or Ryanair)? Such that the goals, strategy, culture, execution, decisions are congruent, holistic and symbiotic?

Judgement. The Bravida Way is a congruent design, but its outcomes are only partly aligned.

The design.

  • Branch managers act as owners with full P&L responsibility.
  • Monthly benchmarking.
  • Margin over volume.
  • Common systems.
  • It produces low fixed costs and fast local decisions.

Where outcomes diverge.

  • EBITA-linked incentives and branch autonomy produced deliberate over-invoicing at Malmö El Service. There were audio recordings of staff discussing it, two convictions, and EY's warning it "might be systematic".
  • eNPS is 14 (target above 20), against Instalco's 31.
  • Unwanted turnover rose to 12.1%. Total turnover is 11.8%, against about 8% historically in the market.
  • Female leaders fell to 10.6% against a 14% target.
  • Code of Conduct training reached 84% completion.
  • Managers have historically chased short-term volume despite the margin-over-volume message.

Against Lifco, Avanza or Ryanair. Bravida has the decentralised structure but not the same congruence between incentives, controls and culture.

For the model. Culture is a risk input:

  • a repeat compliance event;
  • staff loss in a tight labour market;
  • execution slippage in Finland and Denmark, which have the weakest safety metrics (LTIFR 7.0 and 9.8).
C-60

How clear, straightforward and helpful is the company's communication, including on the strategy and the driver transmission mechanism?

C50

Judgement. Communication is clear and helpful on operating momentum. It is opaque on the margin transmission and on large-project economics.

What is disclosed well.

  • Organic growth by country.
  • Order intake and backlog.
  • Installation and service mix by country.
  • Divisional EBITA margins.
  • Twelve-month cash conversion.
  • A clear bridge from reported to adjusted (5.9% adjusted shown beside 7.5% reported).
  • LTIFR by country.
  • Disputes in the notes.

What is opaque.

  • There is no group guidance and no building blocks to the 7% target.
  • Danish basis-point guidance was stopped.
  • There is no margin, milestone or penalty disclosure on over SEK 7bn of data-centre contracts.
  • The figures from the 27 May 2026 Capital Markets Day are not in public evidence.

Narrative-versus-reality gaps flagged in the research.

  • "Stable Swedish service" was claimed while segment service fell 10% in early 2025.
  • Revenue contraction was framed as discipline.

Signalling style. The chief executive tends to talk expectations down ("too early to expect rapid growth in Q2", then +9%). That makes positive surprises more likely than negative ones on volume.

C-61

How strong is the company's record of acting, not just talking?

C64

Question asked How strong is the company's record of acting, not just talking: a recent record of huge delivery and action, and a mid-term track record of delivery, not misses or justifications, whatever the environment (outperforming in tougher ones is even better), beyond its records against guidance and estimates?

Judgement. The record of acting is strong. The mid-term delivery record is mixed: good on revenue, cash and turnarounds; poor on the group margin target.

Recent actions, all delivered in 18 months.

  • The Danish turnaround.
  • The Swedish merger, on time and within its SEK 90 to 100m budget.
  • The ABEKA sale.
  • Two buybacks.
  • The Stavanger court win.
  • Three data-centre awards.
  • The Q1 and Q2 2026 beats on organic growth and margin.

Mid-term.

  • Revenue doubled and dividends rose 14% a year since 2015.
  • EPS rose 16% in FY2025 during a recession.
  • Bravida outperformed Instalco through the downturn. Bravida's margin rose from 5.2% to 5.9% while Instalco's fell to 5.9%, and Bravida's balance sheet stayed at about 1.1x while Instalco's reached 3.3x.
  • Against that: the 7% target missed every year, three of three FY2025 targets missed, and project write-downs in Denmark (2023 to 2024) and Finland (2025).

In tougher environments. Bravida held up better than peers. That is the strongest evidence of quality.

C-62

To what extent does the company hold outlier standards, driving higher, faster and better, with a record of out-executing competitors?

❌ D36

Judgement. Bravida does not hold outlier standards.

  • Margins are below the leading peers: Instalco historically about 8% and 7.1% now, ACG 7.9%, SPIE 7.6%.
  • Culture metrics are below Instalco: eNPS 14 against 31.
  • Safety is mixed: group LTIFR 5.3 near its 5.5 limit.
  • Compliance failed in 2024.
  • The Leadership report rates the chief executive a "weak match for Outlier Standards".

Where it out-executes.

  • It outperformed Instalco in the downturn (above).
  • It won the region's largest data-centre order.

These are out-executions of resilience and capability, not of standards.

For the model. Do not credit Bravida with peer-leading margin conversion. Model it to converge partially toward peers, not to exceed them.

C-63

Explain, with the evidence, the extent to which the company puts shareholders first, ahead of management, and how prudent its governance is: what does the evidence show, and why?

C54

Question asked Explain, with the evidence, the extent to which the company puts shareholders first, ahead of management, and how prudent its governance is (scaffolding put in place even where it was not needed): what does the evidence show, and why?

Judgement. Shareholders are treated fairly, not first. Governance scaffolding was added only after it was needed, which is the opposite of prudent pre-emption.

For shareholders.

  • The payout has been above the 50% policy every year since IPO (63% for FY2025).
  • The dividend has grown 14% a year.
  • Buybacks have started.
  • One share class; no controlling holder.
  • Severance is capped at 12 months, with no severance if the chief executive resigns.
  • Pensions are defined-contribution only.
  • LTIPs require personal investment and three-year EBITA hurdles.

Against them.

  • Chief executive pay is SEK 17.27m, 2.96x Instalco's.
  • Variable pay is up to 125% for the chief executive and 200% for other senior managers, tied mainly to EBITA. That rewards volume and margin, not cash or returns.
  • Directors other than the chief executive own only 70,745 shares, and no insider has bought discretionarily in twelve months.
  • KPMG has been auditor since 2012.

Scaffolding added after the event. The risk council, the uniform risk taxonomy and randomised branch audits all came after Region Skåne. There is still no internal audit function.

Why it looks like this. A widely held company with no anchor owner, run by a long-tenured operator: governance is compliant and reactive rather than owner-driven.

For the model. Expect steady, policy-driven distributions, not aggressive, owner-style capital allocation.

C-64

To what extent is the company an enlightened capital allocator and a dynamic repurchaser of its shares, with a record of allocating capital at a high return on new invested capital?

C52

Question asked To what extent is the company an enlightened capital allocator and a dynamic repurchaser of its shares, not just an operator or generator, with a record of allocating capital at a high return on new invested capital?

Judgement. Bravida is a disciplined acquirer and a conventional distributor. It is not an enlightened or dynamic repurchaser.

Acquisitions: good, not outstanding.

  • 166 deals at 5 to 6x EBITA, integrated within a year, giving mid-teens post-tax returns before synergies.
  • Management walked away from poor targets in 2025 and Q1 2026.

Buybacks.

  • None was made at the late-2025 trough (52-week low SEK 75).
  • The first programme was executed at an average of SEK 115.85 and the second at SEK 141.79: rising, not counter-cyclical.
  • Deutsche Bank called the first "formulaic" and "surprisingly modest".

Other choices.

  • ABEKA was sold for SEK 208m of cash, below the SEK 300m first indicated, at a margin above the group's.
  • ROE declined from 17.4% (2021) to 13.8% (2025).

For the model. Capital returns of SEK 900m to 1.2bn a year (dividend plus buybacks) are the base case. Dynamic, price-sensitive repurchasing should not be assumed.

Risks

❌ D384 questions
C-8075C-8135C-8235C-8325

Group judgement written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

Judgement. The risks are not to the growth engine, which is accelerating. They concentrate in three linked places.

  1. Execution of a new class of large turnkey projects, with the margin and cash risk that brings.
  2. The financing and capex cycle of those projects' customers.
  3. A share price that has re-rated on the same story.

The fundamental downside is moderate in probability and size. A one-in-three chance of a material adverse event over 2027 to 2028, typically worth 5% to 15% of a year's EPS.

The market downside is large. About -26% to -30% to a fair trough multiple on unchanged earnings, and about -35% to -40% if a miss and a de-rating coincide.

For the second set.

  1. Carry an explicit probability-weighted provision or margin haircut on the data-centre contracts.
  2. Model the Danish arbitration as a binary in 2028.
  3. Recognise that, with consensus near the top of the earnings cone, the distribution of outcomes against expectations is skewed down even though the business is improving.
C-80

To what extent has the core growth engine decelerated over the last twelve months: is the revenue growth rate running below the company's own normal rate, and is the input change force itself slowing?

B75

Judgement. The core growth engine has not decelerated. It has accelerated sharply.

Revenue.

  • Organic growth over the last twelve months: -2.5% (Q3 2025), -2.2%, +0.9%, +9.0% (Q2 2026).
  • Reported growth of +9.4% in Q2 2026 is far above the company's normal rate: 5.5% a year for FY2019 to FY2025 including acquisitions, and 2.4% a year for FY2022 to FY2025.

The input forces are strengthening.

  • The construction cycle is still feeding through its lag.
  • Data-centre awards: three in two months.
  • Public budgets.

Three cautions keep this from being an unqualified positive.

  1. The acceleration is mostly base effect. Two-year stacked organic growth was about +0.6% in Q2 2026, and revenue has only regained the 2024 level.
  2. The second engine has slowed sharply. Acquired growth fell from the 5% to 7% target to about 1.6% (2025) and 0% (Q2 2026).
  3. The cyclical input force is past its peak rate of change. Rates are turning up and Swedish construction growth is expected to halve in 2027.

What to watch. The deceleration risk is a 2027 to 2028 risk, not a present one. The first warning would be core (ex data-centre) organic growth below 3% for two quarters in 2027.

C-81

What fundamental risks are there that are sudden, high-magnitude and low-probability, and how plausible and how large is each?

❌ D35

Judgement. Six sudden fundamental risks matter. Two are new since mid-2026 and directly linked to the growth case.

  1. A write-down on a turnkey data-centre contract. The most plausible of the six. - Plausibility: about 20% to 30% for a hit of SEK 100m or more over 2027 to 2028. - Magnitude: SEK 100 to 400m. DNB Carnegie's downside case is above SEK 400m. That is SEK 0.4 to 1.5 of EPS, or 5% to 17% of a year's EPS. - The reasons: first-time principal-contractor scale, penalty clauses, 80% subcontracting, three-year equipment lead times, copper on fixed prices, and Finland's weak delivery record (atNorth sits in Finland).
  2. A customer financing failure, delay or renegotiation. A Nordic co-location developer whose funding tightens in the AI-infrastructure credit crack. - Plausibility: low, about 5% to 10% over 24 months. - Magnitude: work in progress and receivables on one contract (hundreds of SEK millions), plus the loss of future revenue. - Precedent: Northvolt.
  3. Losing the Danish arbitration (SEK 800m; decision in 2028) or a successful Stavanger appeal (about SEK 465m). - Plausibility: low to medium. - Magnitude: a one-off cash and P&L hit of up to about SEK 3 a share pre-tax, which would also trigger a "trapped capital" narrative.
  4. A repeat compliance scandal leading to municipal exclusion. - Public clients are 21% of sales. - Plausibility: low, about 5% to 10%. The controls are new and the 30-customer audit found nothing. - Magnitude: revenue loss over time, plus the multiple.
  5. A supply-chain or subcontractor default chain on a mega-project. Plausibility low; magnitude medium.
  6. Key-person loss of the chief executive. Plausibility low; magnitude high for confidence.

Together. These risks cluster on one exposure: the new large-project book. Because they are correlated, the probability of at least one material adverse event over 2027 to 2028 is meaningful, perhaps one in three. That is the main reason to shade consensus margins.

C-82

What perception- or sentiment-driven risks are there that are sudden and high-magnitude, including events elsewhere in the sector, theme or market that could hit the share price whatever the company's own fundamentals?

❌ D35

Judgement. Perception risk is elevated because the stock has re-rated on a company-specific, theme-linked story.

  • It is up 68% in twelve months.
  • The forward P/E is at the 95th percentile of two years.
  • It now trades partly as an AI-infrastructure beneficiary.

Sudden external triggers.

  1. AI-infrastructure financing or capex news. The environment digest records the first credit crack (CoreWeave bonds below 78 cents). A hyperscaler capex cut, or distress at a Nordic data-centre developer, would hit the stock regardless of delivery.
  2. The Swedish rate turn. A November Riksbank hike, with Sweden's tilt cut to Neutral and its mortgage-reset sensitivity, would hurt the "Swedish turn" narrative that drove the stock.
  3. Peer read-across. Instalco reports Q3 on about 23 October. Bilfinger-style warnings (-44% over twelve months) show how sector contagion spreads.
  4. A media or municipal over-billing story. SVT's "tip of the iceberg" angle.
  5. Regime and positioning. - The environment refuses re-ratings and is Double Underweight on fragility (size, leverage, liquidity). - Bravida is mid-cap (about SEK 31bn) with USD 10 to 13m traded a day. - The industry digest warns that re-rated recovery names now need organic volume to catch up with the price. - Momentum is decelerating: 3-month return +9.6% against 6-month +51.8%.

Magnitude. A sentiment de-rating to the five-year median multiple (12.9x) is about -22% with earnings unchanged. In this sector a sentiment shock on a re-rated name has typically produced 20% to 40% falls.

C-83

How far could the shares fall to a fair trough multiple?

❌ D25

Judgement. A fair trough is about SEK 105 to 110 (-26% to -30%) with earnings intact. It is about SEK 90 to 98 (-34% to -40%) if the trough multiple coincides with a 10% cut to forward earnings, the plausible combined case in this sector.

Inputs.

  • Price SEK 149.0 (16 October 2026).
  • 24-month-forward consensus EPS SEK 9.06.
  • Current multiple 16.5x on the dashboard's basis (FactSet: 16.0x, at the 76th percentile of five years and the 95th of two).

The ladder on consensus earnings.

Multiple Basis Share price Change
14.6x ten-year median about SEK 132 -11%
13.4x ten-year 35th percentile ("conservative") about SEK 121 -19%
12.9x five-year median about SEK 117 -22%
11.8x five-year 25th percentile about SEK 107 -28%
10.9x ten-year 10th percentile ("crash") about SEK 99 -34%
10.1x five-year low about SEK 92 -38%

With an earnings cut.

  • A 10% cut to forward EPS (to about SEK 8.15) is consistent with a 6.6% instead of a 7.0% margin, or with one contract write-down.
  • At 11.8x that gives about SEK 96 (-36%); at 12.9x about SEK 105 (-30%).

Precedents.

  • The 52-week low was SEK 75 (late 2025), at about 10.5x depressed earnings.
  • The Risks report's bear case was 9 to 10x EV/EBITA, about SEK 85.

Why this deserves weight. The premium is company-earned, not sector-driven: Instalco trades at 12.9x (its own 46th percentile) and SPIE at 11.7x. A company-specific miss would remove it.

Guidance

❌ D426 questions
C-6835C-7042C-7145C-7232C-8552C-7345

Group judgement written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

Judgement. Bravida's guidance offers the model almost nothing to lean on.

  • There is no short-term guidance.
  • The undated medium-term targets have been missed on margin for a decade.
  • The building blocks are vague.
  • A management style talks expectations down without ever revising targets.

Against that backdrop, consensus has moved above the company.

  • It is ahead of the targets on growth and cash from FY2026.
  • It is at or above the margin target from FY2027, a level never reached.
  • The company's own signals (informal timelines that slipped, LTIP hurdles that appear about 15% below consensus EBIT) suggest internal planning sits lower.

What this means for upgrades and the model.

  • Upgrades will not come from the company. They will come only from prints beating a consensus that already carries the margin step.
  • Volume beats are likely in H2 2026 to H1 2027. Margin beats against 7.0% in FY2027 are less likely than misses.
  • The second-set model should state its margin assumptions independently and expect consensus margin dispersion to persist.
C-68

To what extent is company guidance above or below sell-side consensus for this financial year and next financial year, and why?

❌ D35

Judgement. Bravida gives no numerical guidance for FY2026 or FY2027. Its only anchors are the undated medium-term targets. Against them, consensus is above the company on growth and cash in both years and on margin from FY2027.

FY2026.

  • Growth: consensus +7.7% (about +9% organic after the loss of about SEK 350m of ABEKA revenue), against a target above 5%. Above.
  • Margin: consensus 6.6% reported, about 6.3% clean, against a target above 7%. Below.
  • Cash: consensus FCF over net income of about 1.3x, against a target above 100%. Above.

FY2027.

  • Growth: +11.5% against above 5%. Above.
  • Margin: 7.0% against above 7%. At the target, which has never been reached.
  • Cash: about 1.2x. Above.

FY2028. A margin of 7.1%, above the target.

Why consensus is ahead.

  1. The brokers model the contracted data-centre revenue, which the targets do not itemise.
  2. Some brokers include the ABEKA gain in FY2026.
  3. The bulls extrapolate Q2 2026 drop-through to the whole increment.

The company's own signals sit lower on margin.

  • Management's informal timeline of "reaching or getting close to" 7% in FY2026 (set in early 2025) has already slipped.
  • The LTIP 2026 full-allocation hurdle of about SEK 2,144m EBITA, if measured on FY2028, sits about 15% below consensus FY2028 EBIT of SEK 2,507m. Verify the measurement basis.

For the model. No guidance protects consensus. Its margin assumptions are the brokers' own and stand above the company's record and, probably, its internal planning.

C-70

Beyond meeting or beating, what is the record, nature and behaviour of the company and its chief executive in upgrading guidance?

❌ D42

Judgement. There is no record of upgrading guidance, because the company deliberately does not guide.

Behaviour.

  • The chief executive avoids specific forward-looking statements and talks expectations down. He did so to Direkt in late 2024, and in May 2026 called it "too early to expect rapid growth in Q2" before a +9% quarter.
  • Management stopped guiding the Danish margin in basis points once the target was hit.
  • Segment guidance, when given, was accurate: Denmark "at or around 5%" delivered 5.0%.
  • Medium-term targets are left unchanged whether they are missed (every year on margin) or approached.

The nature. A company that under-promises in tone and over-promises in targets, but never revises the targets.

For the model.

  • Expect no guidance upgrades as catalysts.
  • Expect positive volume surprises against cautious verbal framing.
  • Expect estimate changes to be driven by the brokers reacting to prints, not by the company.
C-71

How conservative is current guidance for the next one to three years against the last one to five years, adjusted for the difference in input change forces versus the past?

❌ D45

Judgement. Taken together, the guidance is neither conservative nor aggressive. It is conservative on growth and aggressive on margin, and the change forces widen both gaps.

Growth: conservative.

  • The above-5% target compares with revenue growth of 5.5% a year (FY2019 to FY2025, including acquisitions) and 2.4% a year (FY2022 to FY2025).
  • The change forces (contracted data-centre work, a lagged construction recovery) make 7% to 9% likely for FY2026 to FY2027.

Margin: aggressive.

  • Above 7%, against an envelope of 5.2% to 6.9%, a 6.5% peak (FY2021) and 6.2% now.
  • The change forces add volume but also add margin headwinds (wages, copper, mix toward installation, subcontracting).
  • So the margin target is not made easier by the new forces as much as consensus assumes.

Cash: aggressive in the near term and achievable on average.

  • Above 100%, against 77% now and a 99% ten-year average.
  • Achievable in FY2026 to FY2027 if Stavanger and advances arrive; harder in FY2028 to FY2029 as milestones unwind.
C-72

How specific is the guidance: built from stated building blocks, or vague about how results come about?

❌ D32

Judgement. Vague. The company states targets, not building blocks.

Specific items.

  • Restructuring costs of SEK 90 to 100m, and their quarterly phasing.
  • SEK 65m of annual Swedish savings (ramp unspecified).
  • The Danish margin target (now dropped).
  • Dividend and leverage policies.

Missing.

  • Any bridge from 6% to 7%.
  • Timing for the margin target.
  • Data-centre contract margins, phasing and milestone terms.
  • Expected acquisition pace in figures.
  • The Capital Markets Day (27 May 2026) efficiency targets in figures.

For the model. Every margin building block (Finland, Sweden, Norway, data-centre margin, drop-through, procurement) must be built by the analyst. That raises model error and makes consensus dispersion (FY2028 EPS SEK 7.80 to 9.93) unsurprising.

C-85

To what extent is informal or qualitative company commentary above or below formal guidance and sell-side consensus for this financial year and next financial year, and why?

C52

Question asked To what extent is informal/qualitative company commentary above or below formal guidance and sell-side consensus for this financial year and next financial year, and why?

Judgement. Informal commentary is above the formal targets on demand and roughly in line with consensus on volume. It gives no support to consensus on margin above 7% or on the speed of the cash recovery.

Above the targets on demand.

  • Data centres are "the ice on the cake".
  • Defence spending "starting to grow" and providing "a sustained tailwind".
  • Management is "more positive than in previous quarters".
  • "Now prepared to focus more on growth".
  • Bravida is "the leading partner for Swedish hyperscale facilities".

Restrained on margin and cash.

  • No margin timeline is given.
  • On price, the chief executive says growth in this industry comes from cutting prices, and that the company stays with the playbook.
  • The CFO says cash conversion "fluctuates as advances are utilised" with large projects. That implies lumpiness persists.
  • "Too early to expect rapid growth" (May 2026).
  • Southern Sweden is "only gradually improving" (the Head of Sweden).

Why. Management's style is to talk down. Its incentives (annual EBITA bonuses, LTIP EBITA hurdles) reward beating internal plans that appear to sit below consensus.

For the model. Read management's tone as a floor on volume and as no confirmation of the consensus margin.

C-73

What else is material or uncommon about the company's guidance or investor relations?

❌ D45

Judgement. Four uncommon features matter for the model and for reading "consensus".

  1. Consensus is thin and inconsistent. - Six named brokers on FactSet, five for most P&L lines, one for FY2029. - Research is dominated by DNB Carnegie (Buy, SEK 170 to 173) and Deutsche Bank (Hold, SEK 106). - Q1 2026 had three different "consensus" figures: SEK 363m compiled, Deutsche Bank's "2% ahead" basis, and DNB Carnegie's SEK 271m. The same quarter was called a 10% miss and a beat. - Consensus line items do not reconcile (see the preface).
  2. The investor relations function is in transition. A new Head of IR started in September 2026 after an interim period.
  3. The Capital Markets Day of 27 May 2026 reportedly set "definitive capital allocation policies and efficiency targets", but none is in the public evidence base. Obtaining them is a priority.
  4. The ABEKA gain is treated inconsistently across brokers, which distorts FY2026 growth comparisons.

Implication. The second-set model should be anchored on its own bottom-up build. FactSet consensus is a crowd reference with known definitional noise, not a precise benchmark.

Sell-side estimates

❌ D353 questions
C-6738C-6950C-7422

Group judgement written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

Judgement. The sell-side has moved from cutting to raising, and now sits near the top of the plausible outcome range.

Revenue. Consensus FY2026 to FY2027 revenue is well-founded: contracted data-centre work plus a lagged construction recovery. It correctly shows a 2028 to 2029 roll-off.

Margin. Consensus has the margin rising to 7.0% to 7.1%, above anything sustained in ten years. It assumes Q2 2026's roughly 11% drop-through holds on subcontracted mega-project revenue and through a Swedish wage reset.

Cash. Consensus assumes a sharp cash rebound that depends on one court payment and on customer advances.

Revisions. Momentum is positive but cresting: small one- and three-month revisions, a falling buy share, targets at the price.

The record. Bravida beats on volume in upswings and misses on project margin later. The next six to nine months therefore favour revenue beats, while the FY2027 margin carries the miss risk.

For the second set. Use consensus revenue as a reasonable central case for FY2026 to FY2027. Set margin and EPS about 4% to 8% below consensus for FY2027 to FY2028 unless the Q3 and Q4 2026 prints show drop-through above 12% with Finland above 4%. Treat the FY2029 consensus as a placeholder for the refill question.

C-67

How do the sell-side's forecasts for the next one to three years compare with the last one to five years, for the main financial statement line items in particular and the granular working up to them, and how credible is the gap given the change forces and foundations found above?

❌ D38

Judgement. Consensus forecasts a step-change on every line. Revenue is the most credible, margin the least, and below-EBIT and cash in between.

Revenue (most credible).

  • History: FY2022 to FY2025 +2.4% a year; FY2023 +11.9%, FY2024 +0.8%, FY2025 -4.9%.
  • Consensus: +7.7%, +11.5%, +3.6% (FY2026 to FY2028), 7.6% a year, then -4.8% in FY2029 (one broker).
  • By country against an approximate FY2025 base:
  • Sweden +7%, +7%, +2%;
  • Denmark +7%, +6%, +4%;
  • Norway +13%, +21%, +9% (Green Mountain);
  • Finland +15%, +31%, +1% (atNorth).
  • Roughly 40% of the FY2025 to FY2027 increase (SEK 5.7bn) is contracted data-centre work. The remainder implies about 5% to 6% a year of core growth including acquisitions.
  • Credibility: high for FY2026 to FY2027 (backlog 1.34x, contracts, easy comparisons, lagged construction recovery). Moderate for FY2028. The FY2029 decline is the correct signal of the data-centre roll-off.

EBIT and EBITA margin (least credible).

  • History: 6.5% (FY2021), 6.4%, 5.9%, 5.3%, 5.9% (FY2025); 6.2% for the last twelve months.
  • Consensus: 6.6% (about 6.3% clean), 7.0%, 7.1%.
  • EBITDA margin on the brokers' basis: 8.1% (FY2025) to 8.8%, 8.9%, 9.0%. D&A stays flat at about SEK 650m, so EBITDA growth equals EBIT growth.
  • The path needs about 12% drop-through, consistent with Q2 2026. But it must hold on 80%-subcontracted data-centre revenue and through 2027's Swedish wage reset.
  • A segment build (C-75) gives about 6.7% for FY2027 and 6.8% to 6.9% for FY2028: 0.2 to 0.3pp below consensus.

Below EBIT.

  • Net finance: SEK 181m (FY2025) against about SEK 100 to 110m implied by consensus EPS for FY2027 (the pre-tax line implies about SEK 60m).
  • Plausible with lower rates and Stavanger cash, but at risk as the Riksbank turns.
  • Tax is about 21%, credible.
  • Shares are about -0.5% a year, credible.

EPS.

  • History: SEK 6.05, 5.17, 5.88 on the brokers' basis (FY2023 to FY2025), flat over three years.
  • Consensus: SEK 7.20, 8.78, 9.45, 17% a year, then SEK 8.80 (one broker).
  • Credibility: FY2026 is inflated by ABEKA for some brokers. FY2027 is about 4% to 8% above a careful segment build.

Cash.

  • History: operating cash flow SEK 1.42 to 2.17bn (FY2019 to FY2025), FY2025 SEK 1.45bn.
  • Consensus: SEK 2.51bn, 2.51bn, 2.67bn; FCF SEK 2.0 to 2.4bn.
  • Credible for FY2026 only if Stavanger (about SEK 465m) and data-centre advances arrive. Likely front-loaded.

Dividends.

  • Consensus SEK 4.00, 4.25, 4.40 implies a falling payout (56%, 48%, 47%) against a policy of at least 50% and an actual 63%.
  • These lines look stale. Likely DPS is about SEK 4.0 to 4.3 (FY2026) and about SEK 4.6 to 5.2 (FY2027).

Capex: SEK 106m to 155m, in line with history (below 1% of sales).

Net judgement. The revenue gap to history is well-founded in the change forces. The margin gap is founded in a drop-through extrapolation the foundations do not yet prove: no pricing power, little fixed-cost leverage, a ten-year envelope.

C-69

What is the company's record of missing, meeting or beating guidance and sell-side estimates over the last one, three and five years, including in comparable environmental conditions?

C50

Judgement. The record splits by phase of the cycle. Misses were small but persistent through the downturn and its project problems. Beats have come since the volume turn. The targets have never been met.

Last one year (Q3 2025 to Q2 2026).

  • Q3 2025: EBITA miss of about 5% (Swedish pricing, Finnish write-downs).
  • Q4 2025: beat. It lifted 2026 to 2027 consensus EBITA by 3% to 4%.
  • Q1 2026: revenue beat by 3%. EBITA was ambiguous: -10% against a SEK 363m compiled consensus, +2% on Deutsche Bank's basis, +20% against DNB Carnegie.
  • Q2 2026: a clear beat. Adjusted EBITA SEK 452m against SEK 407m (+11%), organic +9% against about +2%.

Last three years (Q3 2023 to Q3 2025).

  • Mostly small EBITA misses of 1% to 6%, against one beat in Q4 2024 (+3%).
  • The FY2025 EPS consensus fell 5.8% over twelve months before delivery. FY2024 EPS (SEK 5.17) was far below early expectations after the Danish write-downs.
  • FY2025 missed all three medium-term targets.

Last five years. The margin target was missed every year, and margin fell from 6.5% to 5.3% before recovering to 5.9%. Dividend and leverage policies were met every year.

Comparable environment.

  • In the last early upswing (2022 to early 2023, organic +16% to +20%), Bravida met or beat (Q4 2022 +12%; the older table's figures need verification).
  • Misses followed in mid-to-late 2023 as contracts priced before the inflation turned unprofitable (Denmark).
  • The present set-up mirrors that sequence:
  • near-term volume beats are likely on easy comparisons and the data-centre ramp;
  • margin misses are the risk from mid-2027, when contracts priced in 2026 meet 2027 wage and copper inflation.

The pattern. Beats come from volume; misses come from project margin, and are usually small, except in write-down episodes (Denmark 2023 to 2024: about -450bp on a segment; Finland Q3 2025).

C-74

To what extent have sell-side estimates been cut deeply and fully across all analysts, and has company guidance recently been rebased lower, or more aggressively kitchen-sinked or sandbagged?

❌❌ F22

Judgement. The opposite set-up applies. Estimates were cut through 2024 to 2025 and have since been raised broadly. There was no guidance to rebase, and none was kitchen-sinked. The "trough-on-trough" window that existed at SEK 75 to 80 in late 2025 has closed.

The cut phase.

  • FY2025 EPS fell from a peak of SEK 6.81 to 5.88 (-5.8% over twelve months).
  • FY2026 bottomed at SEK 6.57.
  • Deutsche Bank trimmed estimates for currency and Denmark.

The raise phase (from February 2026; FactSet mean, to 9 October).

  • FY2027 EPS: +1.8% over one month, +9.0% over three, +15.9% over six.
  • FY2028: +15.6% over six months.
  • Dashboard (FY2027): EPS +3%, +3%, +17%; EBITDA +2%, +4%, +14%; sales +2%, +4%, +14%; targets +5%, +6%, +32% over one, three and six months.
  • No visible broker is cutting.

Momentum is cresting.

  • The three-month and one-month revisions are small.
  • The buy share has fallen 20 points over six months to 60%: three Buy, three Hold.
  • Price targets average SEK 152 (median SEK 153) against a price of SEK 149, so little upside is left on the street.

Sandbagging. The chief executive's tone sandbags volume mildly, but the targets are not sandbagged. For the model, this is an expectations level that has already been rebased up, not down.

Stock market narrative

C561 question
C-2258

Group judgement written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

Judgement. The narrative has shifted from "cyclical installer in a trough" to "structural Nordic AI-infrastructure enabler with a self-help margin story". The shares have priced that shift: +68% in twelve months to a forward P/E of 16.0x to 16.5x, against a ten-year median of about 14.6x.

What the staircase offers.

  • Plausible, dated near-term steps: an easy-comparison Q3 and Q4 2026, a rising backlog, Stavanger cash and more buybacks.
  • A 2027 peak in data-centre revenue.

What it lacks. Novelty. The steps that would be genuinely new (new data-centre awards, a dated margin target, a large distribution) are possible rather than founded.

The negative narrative steps are also dated:

  • the Riksbank in November 2026;
  • the 2027 wage round;
  • 2028 to 2029 contract completions without a refill;
  • the Danish arbitration in 2028.

For the work ahead.

  • The near-term narrative supports volume-led estimate stability.
  • The 18-to-36-month narrative depends on the refill of the data-centre book and on margin delivery, which the model should treat as the two hinges of the case.
C-22

What staircase of novel positive developments lies ahead: which new, dated things can the market look forward to within the next 6 months, in months 6 to 18, and in months 18 to 36; how novel is each; and how well-founded?

C58

Judgement. The staircase is reasonably well-dated and well-founded through 2027, but low in novelty. Most steps are known and partly priced. The novel steps would be new data-centre awards and an extra distribution. Beyond 18 months the staircase turns into completions and a refill question.

Within six months (to April 2027).

Step Date Novelty Foundation
Q3 2026 results 23 October 2026 Moderate Strong (see below)
Second SEK 100m buyback completed 19 October 2026 Low Done
Stavanger cash receipt, about SEK 465m H2 2026, unless appealed Moderate Strong
Possible third buyback Board evaluating to the 2027 AGM Low Moderate
FY2026 results and dividend proposal; possible extra distribution About mid-February 2027 Moderate if an extra distribution Moderate
Swedish national renovation plan End-2026 Low Low for near-term revenue

The Q3 2026 results should be well-founded and are partly novel:

  • comparisons are easy (organic -2.5% and adjusted EBITA SEK 342m in Q3 2025);
  • the data-centre ramp starts;
  • the backlog should rise again with atNorth and EcoDataCenter booked;
  • Finland's margin is the tell.

Months 6 to 18 (April 2027 to April 2028).

  • The peak data-centre revenue year, with the Finnish and Norwegian margins as proof points.
  • The Swedish restructuring complete, with the full SEK 65m run-rate.
  • Finland's margin recovery toward 5%.
  • A resumption of bolt-on acquisitions under the new Head of M&A.
  • Possible new Nordic data-centre awards: the most novel step, but not founded in disclosed pipeline evidence.
  • The 2027 AGM and capital returns.

Negative steps to set against these:

  • Swedish wage settlements in spring 2027 (SEB: +8% minimums).
  • Riksbank hikes.

Months 18 to 36 (to late 2029).

  • Completions: EcoDataCenter (Q1 2028), atNorth (about mid-2028), Green Mountain (2029).
  • The Danish arbitration (2028; SEK 800m): a potential positive cash event, or a write-down.
  • The "above 7%" margin test, dated by brokers to 2027 to 2028.
  • The refill-or-roll-off decision on the data-centre book, which sets FY2029 revenue.

How this lands with the market. The market already has the data-centre story in hand: DNB Carnegie's "let's get structural", and the price up 68%. Each further step needs to be bigger than expected to move the shares. The best-founded positive surprises are near-term: Q3 and Q4 volume, Stavanger cash, distributions.
