Informal commentary:

  • Management talks volume down: "too early to expect rapid growth", May 2026, before organic +9%.
  • It gives no margin timeline. The early-2025 hope of "reaching or getting close to" 7% in FY2026 has slipped.
  • The LTIP 2026 full-allocation hurdle (SEK 2,144m EBITA) most likely refers to FY2026. It is a stretch level about 10% above my FY2026 adjusted EBITA and does not indicate internal planning below or above consensus.
  • A Morgan Stanley preview (16 October 2026) attributes to a Capital Markets Day a roadmap of 4% to 6% organic growth and 20 to 30bp of margin a year. If that is Bravida's, it implies about 6.6% to 6.9% by FY2028, consistent with my 6.55%. The attribution is not verified.

The bridge from the company's own reported figures to the basis consensus uses

  1. Company reported EBITA to company adjusted EBITA. - Adjusted EBITA excludes items affecting comparability. Q2 2026: reported SEK 570m = adjusted SEK 452m + ABEKA gain SEK 158m − restructuring SEK 40m. - For FY2026 I expect reported EBITA of about SEK 2,025m against adjusted SEK 1,942m (+158 gain, −75 restructuring). Add about SEK 145m if the Stavanger interest and cost award is booked as operating income; it may instead go to financial income.
  2. FactSet's history line "EBIT (adjusted)" equals reported EBITA less the ABEKA gain, with restructuring still inside (Q2 2026: SEK 413m). - FactSet's operating EBITDA (FY2025 SEK 2,300m) and the brokers' basis (SEK 2,272m) differ by about 1%. - My adjusted EBITDA for FY2025 on the same construction is about SEK 2,320m.
  3. Brokers' basis. - Each broker's own adjusted figure; FactSet does not state dilution. - For FY2026:
    • DNB Carnegie (SEK 6.96) excludes both the gain and the restructuring;
    • Deutsche Bank (SEK 6.50) includes the restructuring;
    • the July estimates from Oddo BHF (SEK 7.46) and Nordea (SEK 7.27) probably include the gain;
    • Pareto (SEK 7.13) and the restricted broker (SEK 7.10) look clean.
    • The median of SEK 7.20 is therefore about SEK 0.1 above a clean basis. My adjusted SEK 7.11 compares with clean estimates of about SEK 6.96 to 7.13.
  4. EBIT against EBITA. Amortisation of acquired intangibles is close to nil, so consensus EBIT and my adjusted EBITA are comparable. - The consensus median lines do not reconcile with each other. EBITDA SEK 2,689m less D&A SEK 647m is SEK 2,042m, against EBIT SEK 2,002m.
  5. Net financial items. Consensus EBIT less pre-tax profit implies SEK −62m (FY2026), −61m and −67m. My figures are SEK −105m, −120m and −125m; DNB Carnegie's model is about SEK −110m.
  6. Tax. Consensus is consistent with about 21%. Reported FY2026 will be lower because of the tax-free gain.
  7. Shares. I use basic shares (about 204.1m average in FY2026). FactSet's diluted count is 206.4m, about 1% higher, and broker bases are not stated.
  8. Dividends. FactSet's DPS is by fiscal year declared (FY2025 SEK 3.80 matches the company).
  9. Free cash flow. - FactSet's historical "free cash flow" is operating cash flow less capital spending less dividends paid. This is verified for FY2019 to FY2025 (FY2025: 1,453 − 156 − 767 = 530). - Consensus "free cash flow" (SEK 2,014m, 2,162m, 2,406m) is on brokers' own definitions and does not reconcile with consensus operating cash flow less capital spending. - My free cash flow is operating cash flow less capital spending less lease principal, before acquisitions, dividends and buybacks.

What the cross-check rows mean.

  • Earnings per share is adjusted: it excludes the ABEKA gain, the restructuring and any Stavanger interest or cost booking. It is on basic average shares (204.1m, 201.8m, 199.7m, 198.0m, 196.4m) at a 21% tax rate.
  • Reported FY2026 EPS is about SEK 7.6, or about SEK 8.1 if the Stavanger interest and cost award is booked.

  • Free cash flow is operating cash flow after tax, interest and working capital, less capital spending and lease principal, before acquisitions, dividends and buybacks.
  • FY2026 includes about SEK 465m of Stavanger cash: about SEK 320m through working capital and about SEK 145m as interest and costs.

Appendix Research requests the Investment Analyst raised for Layer 3 and Layer 4

Research requests for the next set

Company: Bravida Holding AB (BRAV-SE, Nasdaq Stockholm): a Nordic multi-technical installation and service contractor (electrical, heating, plumbing, ventilation, security, building automation) reporting four country segments (Sweden, Denmark, Norway, Finland), with sales split between project installation (about 52% in FY2025) and recurring service and maintenance (about 48%), now delivering large data-centre contracts for Green Mountain (Norway, through the DC Nordic joint venture with Backe Industri), atNorth (Finland, as principal contractor) and EcoDataCenter (Sweden)

Request: T-D1

Peers: Instalco (INSTAL-SE; the closest listed Nordic decentralised installer, running a Swedish margin recovery under its founder toward an 8% EBITA margin); Assemblin Caverion Group (private, Triton-owned; the Nordic number one, with merger synergies delivered and a data-centre business area set up in Sweden on 31 August 2026); SPIE (SPIE-FR; raised its multi-technical margin from 6.7% in 2023 to 7.6% in 2025 and its 2028 target to 8.0%); Comfort Systems USA (FIX; the clearest precedent of data-centre work lifting an installer's margin); EMCOR Group (EME; large-project data-centre installer); YIT (YIT-FI; general contractor on Finnish data-centre campuses); Bilfinger (GBF-DE; the September 2026 profit warning as a precedent of momentum reversing); Caverion and Eltel (historical large-project write-downs of EUR 59 million and EUR 55 million) Operating conditions: the Nordic construction recovery that began in 2026 (Swedish building starts +8.0% in July 2026) coinciding with renewed rate rises (Riksbank signalling hikes from 1.75% in autumn 2026; Norges Bank at 4.50% after September 2026); the spring 2027 Swedish collective wage round; scarce skilled electricians and project managers; first-time delivery of very large data-centre contracts with about 20% own staff and 80% subcontractors (DNB Carnegie, June 2026); principal-contractor responsibility including civil works (atNorth, Finland, July 2026) Inputs: Can Bravida lift its group EBITA margin above the 5.2% to 6.9% range it has held for ten years, when its medium-term target of above 7% has never been met (peak 6.5% in FY2021, 5.9% to 6.0% in FY2025) and about 90% of its cost base is variable (company, Q1 2020 call; Morgan Stanley, November 2015)? What incremental margin has Bravida earned on returning volume in past up-cycles (2010 to 2011, 2017 to 2018, 2022) against Q2 2026 (adjusted EBITA +SEK 74 million on revenue +SEK 655 million), and how long did elevated incremental margins last after headcount cuts? Has Bravida, or any Nordic installer, delivered a data-centre contract of more than SEK 1 billion as principal contractor, and at what margin? What did Bravida's Finnish unit (EBITA margin 0.6% in Q3 2025, 1.3% in Q1 2026, 3.2% in Q2 2026) need to change, and has any installer turned a sub-scale Nordic unit around while roughly doubling its revenue with a single large contract? Has Bravida's Danish turnaround (0.1% margin in Q2 2024 to 5.1% in Q2 2026) been sustained under volume growth, and how did the Swedish merger savings (SEK 65 million a year, restructuring of SEK 90 to 100 million) ramp? Has Bravida ever restored company-defined cash conversion above 100% within two years of a large-project ramp? How many large data-centre projects can Bravida's central special-projects office (about 70 people) run at once, given a former Bravida Sverige chief executive's view that it can run about two hyperscale projects simultaneously (expert call, 13 July 2026)? Focus: Whether the margin step from about 6% toward 7% rests on strengths Bravida has shown (regional turnarounds, cost flexibility, bid discipline) or on strengths it has not yet shown (group-wide margin above 7%, principal-contracting at scale, a central project organisation able to run several large projects at once). And how often peers that attempted the same step from the same starting point succeeded, and how long it took.

Request: T-D2

Peers: Instalco (INSTAL-SE; same Nordic markets and recovery, fixed-price heavy); Assemblin Caverion Group (private; direct rival for Nordic data-centre awards); SPIE (SPIE-FR; disclosed incremental margins and negative working capital); Comfort Systems USA (FIX; data-centre backlog compounding and margin dispersion); EMCOR Group (EME; working-capital swings on contracts billed ahead); Bilfinger (GBF-DE; sudden margin reversal in 2026); Fasadgruppen (FG-SE; negative operating leverage in Q1 2026); Burkhalter (BRKN-CH; decentralised Swiss installer) Operating conditions: a lagged construction recovery in Sweden, Denmark, Norway and Finland with rates turning up from late 2026; a 2027 Swedish wage round on contracts priced in 2026 without automatic indexation; a concentrated data-centre order book (Green Mountain to early 2029, atNorth to about mid-2028, EcoDataCenter to Q1 2028) for co-location developers whose financing depends on AI-infrastructure credit; a stronger krona against NOK and DKK (53% of sales outside Sweden, unhedged) Inputs: Which few drivers separate Bravida's FY2026 to FY2028 earnings from FY2019 to FY2025 (EPS +5.5% a year, flat in FY2023 to FY2025): the recovery of the core business, the persistence of incremental margins on returning volume, the margin earned on the data-centre contracts, working-capital timing, or the Swedish wage round? How dispersed were outcomes for Nordic and European installers in the two years after a construction upturn began, and how often did a margin miss follow 12 to 18 months after a volume beat (Bravida 2023 is one precedent)? How often did single large contracts produce write-downs for installers in their first years as principal contractor, and what share of annual EBITA did they cost? How does the timing of data-centre revenue recognition shift when switchgear and transformer lead times run at 128 to 160 weeks (Bernstein, October 2026) or when equipment is owner-furnished? How large were working-capital swings when customer advances on large contracts were consumed (Bravida cash conversion 112% in mid-2024, 63% at Q3 2025, 77% at Q2 2026)? How large is the earnings effect of a 10% move in SEK against NOK and DKK (company: about SEK 0.28 of EPS, June 2026)? What is the realistic range of outcomes for the SEK 800 million Danish public-sector arbitration due in 2028? Focus: Whether Bravida's next 18 months rest on one or two drivers (core incremental margin and the atNorth contract) or on many, and how wide and how skewed the range of outcomes has been for comparable installers in the same phase of the cycle.

Request: T-I1

Peers: Instalco (INSTAL-SE; Q3 2026 results about 23 October 2026, the main read-across); Assemblin Caverion Group (private; bond reporting gives margin and order data); SPIE (SPIE-FR; Q3 2026 sales and margin guidance); Bilfinger (GBF-DE; guidance cut in September 2026); Fasadgruppen (FG-SE; Nordic renovation exposure); Comfort Systems USA (FIX) and EMCOR Group (EME) for data-centre order trends Operating conditions: Bravida's Q3 2026 report on 23 October 2026 against easy comparisons (Q3 2025 organic growth −2.5%, adjusted EBITA SEK 342 million); the start of Green Mountain at the end of 2026 and the ramp of atNorth; the last Swedish restructuring charges in Q3 2026; the Stavanger court award (about SEK 320 million plus SEK 145 million of interest and costs, June 2026 ruling, appeal window to early September 2026); Riksbank decisions from November 2026; the spring 2027 Swedish wage agreements Inputs: Bravida gives no short-term guidance and keeps undated targets (growth above 5%, EBITA margin above 7%, cash conversion above 100%, net debt below 2.5x EBITDA, payout at least 50%): is there any sign it will date or change them, or publish the figures reportedly set at its 27 May 2026 Capital Markets Day? How does each broker treat the SEK 158 million tax-free ABEKA gain, the Swedish restructuring and any Stavanger interest income in FY2026 EPS (FactSet median SEK 7.20 on 13 October 2026; DNB Carnegie SEK 6.96, Deutsche Bank SEK 6.50, Oddo BHF SEK 7.46)? What net financial items, tax rate, share count and dividend does each broker assume (FactSet consensus EBIT less pre-tax profit implies about SEK 62 million of net financial cost in FY2026, against about SEK 109 million in FY2025 per DNB Carnegie; consensus dividends SEK 4.00, 4.25 and 4.40 against DNB Carnegie's SEK 4.40, 5.30 and 5.80)? What EBITA margin does each broker assume on the data-centre contracts, and what incremental margin on the core business for FY2027 (consensus EBIT margin 7.0% in FY2027 and 7.1% in FY2028)? What operating cash flow does each broker assume for FY2027 (consensus SEK 2.5 billion against SEK 1.45 billion in FY2025), and how much relies on prepayments and the Stavanger cash? What did Instalco's and SPIE's Q3 2026 reports show about Nordic pricing, wages and incremental margins? What is the record of revisions after Bravida's Q3 and Q4 reports in 2022 to 2025? Focus: Which way FY2027 and FY2028 consensus EBIT, EPS and cash flow are likely to move over the next three reports, given that FY2027 consensus EPS rose 15.9% in six months to 9 October 2026 while the buy share fell 20 points to 60%. And which specific print outcomes would trigger cuts rather than upgrades.

Request: T-I2

Peers: Instalco (INSTAL-SE; shares +49% to +65% over twelve months on the same Swedish recovery); SPIE (SPIE-FR; data-centre and grid narrative with a flat multiple); Assemblin Caverion Group (private; data-centre competitor); Comfort Systems USA (FIX; the US data-centre installer re-rating Bravida bulls cite); Bilfinger (GBF-DE; the sector's de-rating precedent in 2026); Fasadgruppen (FG-SE) and Burkhalter (BRKN-CH; laggards in the same cohort) Operating conditions: a share price up about 68% in twelve months to a 24-month-forward P/E of about 16x (FactSet, 9 October 2026, 95th percentile of two years); a Swedish rate-hiking cycle beginning; the AI-infrastructure financing cycle; private-equity consolidators bidding for bolt-ons (InstallatørGruppen, listed in Copenhagen in June 2026; Nimlas targeting SEK 40 billion of revenue by 2031) Inputs: How widely do sell-side analysts and investors discuss these dynamics for Bravida, and with what view: whether high incremental margins on returning volume will persist after the 4% headcount cut is absorbed; what margin the data-centre contracts earn, and the cost-plus structure of Green Mountain (DNB Carnegie, June 2026); principal-contractor risk on atNorth in Finland; the capacity limit on running several large data-centre projects at once and the refill of the order book before 2028 to 2029 completions; the Swedish 2027 wage round (SEB on minimum wages, Nordea and Swedbank on agreements); the expected reversal of working capital as customer advances are consumed; the scale of buybacks or extra distributions after the Stavanger cash; Assemblin Caverion's entry into Nordic data centres; operators buying equipment directly from manufacturers; the Danish arbitration in 2028; the definitional noise in consensus (the ABEKA gain, restructuring and net financial items); the LTIP 2026 EBITA hurdle of SEK 2,144 million and its measurement year? Which of these are discussed by DNB Carnegie, Deutsche Bank, Pareto, Nordea, Oddo BHF and ABG Sundal Collier, and which appear in investor commentary (for example on X or Swedish investor forums), since when, and how prominently? Focus: Which of the dynamics that decide Bravida's FY2027 to FY2028 earnings (core margin persistence, data-centre margin and refill, FY2027 working capital) are already in the narrative, and which are discussed by one or two analysts or by no one.

Layer 3 and Layer 4

Layer 3: Assessing the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months
and Layer 4: Assessing the extent to which our assessment of the foundational components and financial forecasts is reflected or not in current company guidance, sell-side estimates and stock market narrative, primarily for the next 18 months and secondarily for 18 months to 3 years out

10 questions in 4 groups · 13,492 words · written 22 October 2026, 11:44 to 12:02 UK, Fable, main login

What the Investment Analyst was asked

The objective is to produce a robustly tested assessment of the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months, based on the first and second sets' answers.

The objective is to produce that judgement, based on the first and second sets' answers and your answers to the third set, for the Investment Analyst who writes the investment memo, so that Richard, who reads the memo, can form his judgement on the investment case.

What the scores mean in this layer

Finish each answer and each judgement on a group or set with a score from 1 to 100; a higher number is more positive for an investment case in the company.

For the third set's questions, the score says how positive the thing the question asks about is for the predictability of the company's financial forecasts over the next 18 months, and so for an investment case in the company: 100 the most positive, 1 the most negative, 50 neither.

For the fourth set's questions, the score says how favourable for an investment case in the company the gap is between our assessment and what current guidance, sell-side estimates and the stock market narrative already reflect: 100 the most favourable, 1 the least, 50 neither.

The Investment Analyst's opening notes for this layer

The Investment Analyst's title
Bravida (BRAV-SE): third and fourth sets of questions, the predictability of the forecasts and what the market already reflects

Written 2026-10-22 for the Investment Analyst who writes the investment memo. Money in SEK millions unless stated; per-share figures in SEK. "Adjusted EBITA" and "adjusted EPS" are as the second set defined them: before the SEK 158m ABEKA gain, the Swedish restructuring charges and any booking of the Stavanger interest and cost award. "The model" means the Financial Analyst's model after model update 1. "Consensus" means the FactSet median unless a broker is named. Bravida reports Q3 2026 tomorrow, 23 October 2026, and Instalco reports the same day; nothing below uses either report, and I say where each would change a judgement.

Before the answers: what the research resolved, where I differ from the earlier sets, and the model cross-check

What the six commissioned reports and the four template reports resolved

The second set left eleven verification items. Nine are now settled. Four change a judgement; none changes a forecast input.

  1. The "Capital Markets Day roadmap" was never Bravida's. The figures Morgan Stanley's 16 October 2026 preview was thought to attribute to Bravida (10% EPS growth a year, 4% to 6% organic growth, 20 to 30bp of margin a year) are Securitas's June 2026 targets (DNB Carnegie, 18 June 2026; the Morgan Stanley note lists them under Securitas). Bravida's Capital Markets Day of 27 May 2026 reconfirmed its five undated targets and added no figures, no dates and no building blocks. So there is no quantified anchor from the company at all. Every margin path in the market is a broker's construction, and ours is ours.
  2. The incentive hurdles are single-year levels, not planning figures. The SEK 1,855m, 1,965m and 2,144m full-allocation EBITA levels belong to LTIP 2022, 2023 and 2024 and are measured on normalised group EBITA for FY2024, FY2025 and FY2026 respectively (annual report 2025, note 5). Normalised EBITA excludes the ABEKA gain and adds back restructuring. Nothing vests below 77% of the level (lowered from 85% at the 2025 AGM); LTIP 2022 paid out 31.9% of maximum. The model's FY2026 adjusted EBITA of SEK 1,935m is 90% of the FY2026 hurdle, so partial vesting. The second set's reading (a stretch level above outcomes, saying nothing about internal planning) is confirmed, and the first set's inference that the board's ambition sits below consensus is dropped for good.
  3. The Stavanger award is final. The appeal window closed in early September 2026 without an appeal (DNB Carnegie, 8 September 2026; the Q2 call had set the deadline). The cash (about SEK 320m principal plus about SEK 145m interest and costs) had not been confirmed as received by the last filing; the Q3 report should show it in trade receivables and in cash. The interest and cost element is expected to pass through financial income or as a reduction of operating costs; the model treats it as an item affecting comparability, which stands.
  4. The Danish exposure is smaller than SEK 800m. The SEK 800m was two receivables. One (the Niels Bohr University case) was settled in Q4 2025 with about SEK 100m received, "in line with assessments". The remaining hospital dispute goes to arbitration in 2028, with no provision taken and management expecting no significant earnings effect. The maximum one-off hit is therefore about SEK 2.5 to 2.7 a share after tax, not the SEK 3.1 the second set carried. Precedents cut both ways: Peab took seven years to collect a SEK 1.4bn Danish-style award; Coor lost a Danish arbitration in Q3 2026 for up to SEK 25m.
  5. There is no factoring. The 2025 annual report and the Q2 2026 report disclose no non-recourse factoring or receivables sale; trade receivables are held at amortised cost. Reported cash conversion is organic. The second set's flag is cleared.
  6. The atNorth role is narrower than both earlier sets framed it. Bravida is principal contractor "for all installation work and management of the construction contract" at the Kouvola FIN04 campus (press release, 9 July 2026). The civil construction at the same campus is contracted separately: YIT holds a EUR 300m design-build contract for shell and core (21 July 2026) and Peab is building contractor for EUR 130m (Peab Q2 2026 report); NRC Group builds the substation (EUR 25m, to May 2028). Bravida does not pour the concrete. What it carries is the coordination, schedule and interface liability of a principal contractor, for the first time at this scale. That is still the main execution risk in the book, but it is a management risk, not a civil-works risk, and the second set's SEK 25m a year allowance is the right order.
  7. The 25 August 2026 award is real and probably a fourth contract. The press release "Bravida awarded installation contract for a new data center in Sweden worth approximately 850 MSEK" is cited by title and date in four separate reports, and one places it in Mälardalen, where DNB Carnegie's "undisclosed client" (which it suggests is Amazon Web Services) operates. One report conflates it with the two EcoDataCenter orders in Borlänge (SEK 200m in 2025 plus SEK 650m in July 2026, which also sum to SEK 850m). On balance it is distinct; I keep the second set's half weight because the conflation is unresolved and full weight adds only SEK 100m (0.3%) to FY2027 revenue.
  8. Customer financing. atNorth's acquisition by Equinix and CPP Investments completed on 8 September 2026 (confirmed). Green Mountain's disclosed financing (EUR 371m, Jefferies, December 2024) relates to its Hamar platform, whose anchor tenant is TikTok (Morgan Stanley, 15 July 2025); the tenant and financing of the Stavanger site Bravida is building are not in evidence. Green Mountain remains the least-evidenced of the three customers.
  9. Veidekke's market figures are verified (21 October 2026): Swedish construction production +9% in 2026 and +4% in 2027; the Scandinavian contracting forecast for 2026 raised from 6% to 7%, led by public buildings and infrastructure. The first set's vintage caveat is cleared.

Two items stay open: the DC Nordic joint venture's consolidation (Bravida books its SEK 4.3bn share in its own order intake, which implies proportional treatment but does not prove it), and the exact prepayment and milestone terms on Green Mountain and atNorth (undisclosed; management gave only a generic 5% to 15% advance range for projects).

Where I differ from, or sharpen, the earlier sets

  • The working capital proxy does not include customer advances. The second set said FactSet's working capital line (receivables plus inventories less payables) is used "as the proxy for total operating working capital, including customer advances". It does not include them. The quarterly components prove it: at Q2 2026, trade receivables 6,027 plus contract assets 2,935 less trade payables 2,426 equals 6,536, exactly FactSet's figure; contract liabilities (customer advances) of 3,324 are outside it. The same holds at Q2 2025 (5,355 + 3,765 − 2,227 = 6,893). The input still works, because the second set set the percentages by judgement as a net proxy (19.5% in FY2026 for the Stavanger receipt and the advances; 20.0% in FY2027 for their consumption), but the Consumer should monitor advances directly, through the contract liabilities line in the notes, and should know that the model cannot show an advance inflow and its consumption separately.
  • The consensus gap can now be decomposed, and it is more concentrated than the second set said. DNB Carnegie's base case assumes a 10% EBITA margin on the data-centre contracts and a 7.0% margin on the rest of the business in FY2027 to FY2028 (8 September 2026). Applied to the model's volumes, those two assumptions would put FY2027 EBITA about SEK 300m above the model's SEK 2,176m: about SEK 157m from the data-centre margin (10% against 5.25% on SEK 3.3bn) and about SEK 125m from the core margin (7.0% against 6.59% on SEK 30.6bn). The gap to the consensus median (SEK 2,370m) is SEK 194m. If the other brokers share DNB Carnegie's data-centre assumption, roughly half of the consensus gap sits in one undisclosed number, the contract margin on work whose largest piece is cost-plus. The second set put 80% to 90% of the gap on "margin persistence plus the data-centre margin" without splitting them; the split matters because the two are tested by different evidence at different dates.
  • Finland's margin path now rests on atNorth, not on the Finnish core. Finnish building permits are up 17% in 2026 to date but down 6% excluding data centres (DNB Carnegie, 15 October 2026). The second set modelled the Finnish core recovering from about 1.5% to 4.5% with atNorth as the risk to that path. I read it the other way round: the core has no volume recovery to lift it, and the segment reaches 4% to 4.5% only if atNorth earns at or above 5%. The segment is 8% of sales (one point of Finnish margin is about SEK 28m, 0.08pp of group margin), so the input stays; the monitoring point changes.
  • The 2027 wage round: the second set's size is right, the first set's mechanism is right. Expected actual wage growth is 3.2% (Swedbank), 3.2% to 3.4% (the Riksbank) and just below 3.0% (Nordea); SEB's 8% is the rise in agreed minimum scales, well below what skilled installers earn. But the 2023 precedent is real: on the Q3 2023 call management said it could not pass on all cost increases, and the Q2 2023 margin fell 30bp on +5% organic growth. The company's own sensitivity is SEK 100m of pre-tax profit per 1% of payroll. The central SEK 25m squeeze stands; the tail (settlements above 4%, or drift above the agreements in the north) is SEK 60 to 80m.
  • Cash conversion has been restored before, twice. Company-defined cash conversion fell to 57% in Q3 2023 (Stockholm Bypass advances consumed) and was 105% for FY2024; it was 115% in 2019 after the 2018 Bypass award. Both recoveries came within two years of the ramp. That is direct support for the model's FY2028 recovery to 93% to 100% after the FY2027 trough, and the first set's "cash flow timing illusion" is better described as a documented two-year cycle.

The model cross-check

The summary shows no year flagged, and no gap is material. For the record:

  • Earnings per share differ by SEK 0.03 to 0.07 (under 1%) in every year. The cause is that the model takes EBITDA margins rounded to one decimal place (8.5%, 8.4%, 8.6%, 8.7%, 8.6%) while the second set's own figures used 8.53%, 8.45%, 8.55%, 8.66% and 8.64%; on SEK 30 to 37bn of revenue each 0.05pp is SEK 15 to 18m of EBITA. The sign alternates with the rounding. No input is wrong.
  • Free cash flow differs by SEK 78m (5%) in FY2026 and by SEK 13 to 16m in later years. The FY2026 gap is the second set's own arithmetic: its cross-check implicitly held working capital flat in FY2026, whereas the input (19.5% of revenue) applied to FY2026 revenue of SEK 30,408m gives a balance of SEK 5,930m against SEK 5,867m at end-2025, a SEK 63m outflow; the rest is tax and interest timing inside the model. The input is right: on the FactSet measure, the Stavanger principal takes about SEK 320m out of receivables while 7.8% more revenue adds about SEK 450m, so a small outflow on that measure is the correct reading, with the advances arriving outside it. No input is wrong.

I change no forecast input. The research confirms the second set's base case on every line it touched; where it moved a figure (the Danish exposure, the atNorth civil scope, the SEK 850m award) the effect on the five-year lines is below SEK 0.05 of EPS, which would not change any rating or judgement.

The third set: assessing the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months

Layer 3: Assessing the predictability of the financial forecasts for the next 18 months

Risks

❌ D477 questions
D-0166D-0258D-0352D-0448D-0555D-0650D-0738

Group judgement written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

Judgement. The financial forecasts for the next 18 months are predictable in their revenue and their below-the-operating-line items, moderately predictable in their margin up to about 6.6%, and unpredictable in their cash in FY2027 and in whether the margin goes beyond 6.6%. The seven questions converge on one structure:

  • The earlier sets agree with each other, and the research agrees with them (D-01). The corrections are of size, not direction, and three of them (the atNorth civil scope, the Danish exposure, the absence of factoring) reduce risk rather than add it.
  • The model's case rests on demonstrated strengths; consensus's does not (D-02). The 8% to 10% EPS gap in FY2027 to FY2028 is the gap between a package contractor's margin and a principal contractor's premium, and between a 6.5% group margin and one never reached.
  • Earnings drivers are diffuse, risk drivers are concentrated (D-03). Three-fifths of the EPS gain is a four-country cyclical recovery; the three variables that set the cone are two undisclosed margins and one binary project outcome.
  • The cone is narrow for revenue, moderate for EPS, wide for cash, and skewed down against consensus (D-04). Upside arrives early and small; downside arrives late and large.
  • Disclosure lets the Consumer watch the core and the cash, not the data-centre margin (D-05), which is the one thing that would resolve the consensus gap.
  • The transmission is clear everywhere except where it matters most (D-06): the contract margin and the milestone schedule.
  • The share price and multiple are 90% idiosyncratic and at the 95th percentile of two years (D-07), so the multiple is a second bet on the same undisclosed margin.

The steer. Write the memo's forecast as two cases with a named hinge. Case one, the model: SEK 8.0 to 8.1 of FY2027 EPS, revenue in line with consensus, margin 6.4% to 6.5%, a cash trough in FY2027; this is predictable to within about ±10% on earnings and is what demonstrated delivery supports. Case two, consensus: SEK 8.8, margin 7.0%, cash SEK 2.5bn; this needs a 10% data-centre margin and a 7% core, neither observable before mid-2027 and neither ever reported. The hinge is the data-centre contract margin, and the first external reads on it are the Finnish and Norwegian segment margins in the Q4 2026 and Q1 2027 reports. Until then, the predictable statement is about direction: estimates are more likely to come down to the model than the model is to go up to estimates, and the timing of that is the first half of 2027. The next two prints (Q3 and Q4 2026) will look strong on volume and cash for reasons that are already known, and should not be read as evidence on the hinge.

D-01

How congruent are the answers to all the questions in the earlier sets?

C66

Question asked How congruent are the answers to all the questions in the earlier sets: to what extent do they support and align with each other, rather than contradict or jar?

Judgement. The two sets are congruent on the shape of the case and differ only in the size of individual items, and the research now settles most of those differences in the second set's favour. Congruence is high where it matters for predictability: both sets say revenue to FY2027 is the predictable part, margin is the variable, cash is front-loaded and the data-centre book is the new risk class. The one structural tension that remains is not between the sets but between both sets and the most-followed broker: on what the data-centre contracts earn.

Where the sets align, and the evidence now behind the alignment.

  • Revenue is predictable and consensus has it right. First set: contracted data-centre work plus a lagged construction recovery justify consensus revenue; second set: revenue within 1% of consensus to FY2027, confidence "high". The Q2 2026 order book (SEK 20.4bn, 1.34 times installation sales), Veidekke's verified 2026 to 2027 path and the four awards support both.
  • Margin follows volume only while slack lasts. First set: fixed-cost leverage is low, about 84% of revenue is variable production cost, 0.5pp of margin matters five times more than 1% of volume; second set: incremental margin fades through 2027 as the 4% headcount cut is absorbed. Management's own 8% to 9% fixed-cost statement (Q1 2021 call) and the FY2022 precedent (revenue +20%, margin flat) back both.
  • Cash is front-loaded, then weak, then normal. First set's "timing illusion" and the second set's FY2026 strong, FY2027 weak, FY2028 normal path are the same statement; the contract liabilities history (4,403 at Q2 2025 to 3,324 at Q2 2026, as the earlier advance was consumed) and the two restorations above 100% within two years are the evidence.
  • The data-centre book raises growth and risk at once. Both sets: first principal-contractor role at scale, 80% subcontracted labour, three to four customers holding about 30% of the backlog. Confirmed, with the civil scope narrower than framed (preface item 6).

Where the sets differed, and how it resolves.

Item First set Second set Resolution
2027 wage squeeze 20 to 40bp about 7bp (SEK 25m) Second set, on the three bank forecasts; the first set's mechanism (the 2023 lag) is the tail case
Net financial cost SEK 181m, consensus SEK 60m "too light by SEK 0.5" SEK 105 to 125m; consensus light by SEK 0.15 to 0.25 Second set (DNB Carnegie models SEK 93 to 110m)
LTIP hurdle Board ambition 15% below consensus A stretch level on FY2026; no planning signal Second set, confirmed by the annual report's note 5
Customer financing A crack in the AI financing cycle threatens the book Lower for atNorth and EcoDataCenter; real for future awards Second set; atNorth's new owners confirmed
Largest contract's margin risk Fixed-price exposure to copper and penalties Green Mountain is cost-plus; risk concentrates in atNorth Second set; and atNorth's civil scope sits with YIT and Peab
Clean FY2026 EPS SEK 6.5 to 6.9 about SEK 7.1 Second set (DNB Carnegie SEK 7.00 adjusted, 7.37 reported)
Capital Markets Day figures Unknown, obtain them A possible roadmap, unverified Neither: the roadmap is Securitas's; the Day gave no figures

What still jars.

  1. The sets against DNB Carnegie on the data-centre margin. Both sets model 5% to 6%; DNB Carnegie models 10%, and consensus margins of 7.0% to 7.1% imply most brokers are nearer DNB Carnegie than us. This is the single point where our model and the market's model disagree on a fact that neither can observe, and it will stay unobservable: the company does not report the Special Projects office as a segment.
  2. The first set's "the crew leaks the scarcity margin" against DNB Carnegie's "20/80 subcontracting is not margin-dilutive". Both can be true: subcontracting protects the fixed cost base (DNB Carnegie's point) and caps the margin at a fee on managed labour (the first set's point). The second set's 6% before allowance is the reconciliation.
  3. The second set's working capital definition (preface) was wrong in words though right in effect.
  4. Small factual inconsistencies that do not matter for the forecasts: the Q2 2026 backlog growth is given as +21% (first set) and +25% (one AlphaSense report); the Finnish Q2 2025 margin is given as 2.7% in the Q2 2026 report against the first set's lower figures; FY2025 reported EBITA is SEK 1,668m (company) against "about 1,690 adjusted" (second set), the difference being the SEK 20m Q4 2025 restructuring charge.

Why this matters for predictability. A case whose sets disagree on direction is unpredictable because the analyst does not know which lens to use. Here the lenses agree and the disagreements are on magnitudes that the next two prints will narrow (the wage round excepted, which lands in spring 2027). The residual risk from incongruence is small; the residual risk from the one shared blind spot (the data-centre margin) is not, and it is treated in D-03 and D-04.

D-02

To what extent does past and present fundamental delivery make the expected change plausible, and to what extent does the thesis rely only on strengths the business demonstrably has, rather than on strengths it would need to acquire?

C58

Judgement. Split the "expected change" into the two versions that exist. The model's version (adjusted EPS SEK 7.1, 8.1 and 8.5 for FY2026 to FY2028; margin 6.4% to 6.6%) rests almost entirely on strengths Bravida has demonstrated in the last 30 months, and is therefore plausible with ordinary execution. Consensus's version (SEK 7.2, 8.8 and 9.5; margin 6.6% rising to 7.0% and 7.1%) rests on two strengths the business has never shown: a group margin held above 6.5%, and double-digit margins on data-centre work delivered as principal contractor. The gap between the two versions is the gap between demonstrated and required strengths, and it is about 8% to 10% of FY2027 to FY2028 EPS.

Strengths demonstrated, and what each carries in the model.

  1. Volume returns to a cut cost base and the margin rises. Demonstrated in H1 2026: organic +0.9% then +9.0%, adjusted EBITA +16% in H1 and +20% in Q2, incremental margin about 11% (9% on the core after the savings and the ABEKA loss). Carries about 60% of the FY2025 to FY2028 EPS gain (volume at a constant margin).
  2. Bounded regional turnarounds. Denmark from 0.1% (Q2 2024) to 5.1% (Q2 2026) and, the test that matters, held at 5.1% while growing 11% organically in Q2 2026. Norway 2013 to 2014 under the same chief executive. The Swedish merger delivered on time and inside its SEK 90 to 100m budget; the SEK 65m saving is the kind of specific, dated programme the company has always delivered. Carries about 0.3pp of the 0.55pp margin gain.
  3. Data-centre installation as a package contractor. Since 2009 (Meta at Luleå, with retrofits worth 1.5 times the original contract), eight projects for EcoDataCenter since 2017, Skygard in Oslo (2025), repeat work for XTX Markets at Kajaani under YIT as main contractor. Carries the SEK 3.3bn of FY2027 data-centre revenue at a 5% to 6% margin, which is a package contractor's margin, not a principal contractor's premium.
  4. Restoring cash conversion within two years of a large-project ramp. 115% in 2019, 105% in 2024. Carries the FY2028 recovery.
  5. Bid control and selectivity. Chief-executive sign-off above SEK 50m; the FY2025 margin rose 0.6pp on revenue −4.9% by walking away from volume; southern Swedish branches closed. Carries the assumption that the core margin does not erode in 2026 to 2027.
  6. Capital returns. Two SEK 100m programmes executed, dividend grown every year since listing. Carries the 1% a year of EPS from the share count.

Strengths not demonstrated, and who relies on them.

  1. A group margin above 6.5%. Never in ten years (peak 6.5% in FY2021; 5.1% to 6.3% over 2009 to 2014 per Morgan Stanley's initiation). Consensus has 7.0% in FY2027. The model never reaches 7% in five years. Peers show the level is reachable (SPIE 6.7% to 7.6% in two years through selectivity and mix; Assemblin Caverion 7.9% to 8.5% after integration) and also that it is lost as fast as gained (Instalco from about 8% in 2021 to 5.8% in 2025 on cost inflation and fixed-price exposure). Of the three listed peers that attempted a 100bp step from a similar base in the last five years, one succeeded (SPIE), one reversed (Instalco) and one collapsed (Bilfinger, 6.0% to 3.2% to 3.6% guided in September 2026).
  2. Principal contracting above SEK 1bn. No listed Nordic installer has done it (AlphaSense, October 2026). The atNorth scope is narrower than feared (preface item 6), but the coordination role is new. The model carries SEK 25m a year of allowance and a 20% to 30% chance of a SEK 100 to 400m problem over two years; consensus carries, on DNB Carnegie's figures, a 10% margin and no allowance.
  3. Running two mega-projects at once with a 70-person central office. The Stockholm Bypass (SEK 2.7bn, 2018) was one project at a time. A former Bravida Sverige chief executive puts the capacity at two hyperscale projects; DNB Carnegie argues local organisations can be drawn on. Green Mountain and atNorth now test this for the first time, together, from late 2026. The model needs this to work only at 5% to 6%; consensus needs it to work at 10%.
  4. Pricing above cost. Not demonstrated (gross margin 14.2% to 15.2% for seven years). The model assumes none; consensus's 7.0% core margin needs some, unless cost-out beyond the SEK 65m is found.

The precedent that fits the sequence. In the last upswing Bravida beat on volume (Q1 2023: organic +20%, margin stable at 5.0%) and missed on margin two to three quarters later (Q2 2023: −30bp on +5%; Danish write-downs through 2023 to 2024). The present sequence (volume beats in Q1 and Q2 2026; contracts priced in 2026 meeting 2027 costs; a new project class) mirrors it. That is why the model's lean is down on margin from mid-2027 and why the group margin step is the undemonstrated strength the Consumer should weight least.

Net. The model's expected change is plausible on demonstrated strengths with one caveat: the data-centre book must be delivered at a package contractor's margin without a write-down, which is probable but not demonstrated at this scale. Consensus's expected change needs the undemonstrated strengths, and the record says those arrive late or not at all.

D-03

In relation to the differences between the forecasts over the next 18 months to four years and the last four to six years' financials, how diffuse or concentrated are the key drivers that generate the differences?

C52

Question asked In relation to the differences between the forecasts over the next 18 months to four years, vs. the last four to six years' financials, how diffuse or concentrated are the key drivers that generate the differences?

Judgement. The drivers of the upside are diffuse; the drivers of the risk are concentrated. The FY2025 to FY2028 EPS gain (about SEK 2.4 to 2.5 a share) is spread across a four-country cyclical recovery, three regional margin programmes, a contracted project book and a share count, and no single one of them is worth more than a fifth of it. The outcomes that would make FY2027 differ from the base case by more than 10%, in either direction, sit in three places: the contract margin on the data-centre book, the incremental margin on the core, and the timing of working capital. Two of those three are unobservable from outside until they have happened. That combination, diffuse earnings and concentrated risk, is what makes the case predictable in revenue and unpredictable in the second decimal of the margin.

The difference to history, decomposed. The last six years gave revenue growth of 5.5% a year and EPS growth of 5.5% a year, with a flat margin envelope and EPS flat over FY2023 to FY2025. The model's FY2025 to FY2028 gives revenue growth of 7.2% a year and EPS growth of about 12% a year (from the brokers' SEK 5.88). The sources of the extra EPS (from the second set, using its clean FY2025 base of about SEK 6.1 to SEK 8.51):

Driver Contribution to the gain Share How concentrated
Core volume at a constant 6.0% margin (four countries, about 330 branches, 84,000 to 91,000 customers) about +1.1 about 45% Diffuse
Data-centre contract revenue at a 5% to 6% margin about +0.4 about 15% Concentrated: three to four contracts, two customers for 85% of it
Swedish savings (SEK 65m) about +0.25 about 10% One programme, contracted
Finland from about 1.5% to about 4.5% about +0.28 about 11% One unit, one contract inside it
Denmark and Norway edging up; recovery-phase incremental margin net of mix dilution about +0.2 about 8% Two units plus a group-wide mechanism
Buybacks about +0.2 about 8% One board decision a year
Interest and price lag about −0.08

Against the last four to six years, then, three-fifths of the change is the cycle acting on the whole branch network, which is the most diffuse driver an installer can have. The margin step is three named programmes, each small, each trackable, each with a precedent.

Where the range comes from (FY2027). The second set's cone (EPS SEK 7.0 to 9.0) is generated almost entirely by three variables:

  1. The data-centre contract margin. Each point on SEK 3.3bn is SEK 33m, SEK 0.13 of EPS. The plausible range is 4% (one problem contract offsetting the rest) to 9%; DNB Carnegie's 10% is above it. The whole range is SEK 0.65 of EPS. Cost-plus on the largest piece (Green Mountain) narrows the downside and caps the upside; atNorth's terms are undisclosed; YIT's data-centre work "exceeds" its 6% contracting target, which is the best external anchor for a Nordic contractor's margin on this work.
  2. The core incremental margin. Each two points of drop-through on the roughly SEK 2.5bn of core revenue added across FY2026 to FY2027 is about SEK 50m, SEK 0.19 of EPS; the range (6% to 14%) is about SEK 0.75 of EPS. This is the variable with the most direct read in the next two prints (the Swedish and Danish segment margins year on year).
  3. A single project outcome. A problem contract of SEK 100 to 400m is SEK 0.4 to 1.5 of EPS in the year it lands, at a 20% to 30% chance over two years. This driver has no middle: it is zero or large.

Secondary variables are small on their own: the wage round (central −SEK 25m, tail −SEK 60 to 80m, SEK 0.1 to 0.3), currency (SEK 0.28 per 10% move against NOK and DKK, unhedged), interest (SEK 0.06 per point), Finland's core (SEK 0.11 per point).

Working capital is a concentrated driver of cash, not of earnings. The FY2027 operating cash flow range (the model SEK 1.46bn, consensus SEK 2.51bn) is set by one mechanism: the consumption of the Green Mountain and atNorth advances. It is the largest percentage gap between the model and consensus anywhere, and it depends on milestone terms nobody outside the company has seen.

How this compares with the 2019 to 2025 drivers. The last cycle's differences were made by the Stockholm Bypass (one project), the Danish write-downs (one unit) and the Northvolt loss (one customer). The concentration of risk in one project and one unit is therefore not new to Bravida; it is the company's normal risk shape. What is new is the size: SEK 7 to 8bn of awards against SEK 2.7bn for the Bypass, and 30% of the backlog against perhaps 10%.

Against the AlphaSense reading. The commissioned report calls driver concentration "extremely high". I disagree for the earnings and agree for the risk. The report reaches its conclusion by listing the risks (contracts, arbitration, currency, wages) and not the earnings; the memo should keep the two apart, because the policy response differs: diffuse earnings drivers argue for confidence in the base case, concentrated risk drivers argue for a wide cone around it.

D-04

How wide is the cone of plausible outcomes over the next 18 months, how skewed is it, and so how predictable is the case?

❌ D48

Judgement. The cone is narrow for revenue, moderate for adjusted EPS and wide for cash, and its skew depends entirely on the reference point. Around the model's base case the FY2027 EPS cone (SEK 7.0 to 9.0, −14% to +11%) is close to symmetric, with the write-down tail on one side and the volume-and-distribution upside on the other. Around consensus (SEK 8.78) the same cone is skewed firmly down: consensus sits at about the 75th to 80th percentile of plausible outcomes, and the probability-weighted figure is about SEK 8.1. So the case is predictable in the sense that matters for the memo (the direction of surprise against expectations is known) and unpredictable in the sense that matters for a holder (the size of a single project outcome is not).

The cone, line by line, to end-2027.

Line Base (model) Plausible range What sets the width Skew against consensus
Revenue FY2027 33,936 33,000 to 34,500 (−3% to +2%) Core organic 2% to 6%; data-centre phasing (equipment lead times: 18 to 24 months for medium-voltage transformers, 138 weeks for high-voltage, Bernstein, June 2026; owner-furnished equipment mitigates); currency Balanced; consensus 33,889 is at the centre
Adjusted EBITA margin FY2027 6.41% 5.8% to 7.0% Data-centre margin 4% to 9%; core drop-through 6% to 14%; wage round Down: consensus 7.0% is the top of the range
Adjusted EPS FY2027 8.04 7.0 to 9.0 As above plus one project outcome Down: consensus 8.78 near the 78th percentile
Operating cash flow FY2027 1,455 1,100 to 2,100 Advance consumption; milestone approvals; a refill award with new advances Strongly down: consensus 2,506 is outside the range unless new prepaid awards arrive
Dividend for FY2026 (paid 2027) 4.20 4.00 to 4.50 Board convention (payout 51% to 69% since listing; 63% last year) Up: consensus 4.00 is the bottom
Reported EPS FY2026 about 7.6 to 8.1 7.4 to 8.2 Whether the Stavanger interest award is booked in operating or financial income Up against brokers who exclude both items; the "beat" will be partly definitional

Why the revenue cone is narrow. About SEK 3.3bn of FY2027 revenue is contracted and dated; the core order book covers about eight months; comparisons are easy through Q4 2026; and the chief executive's habit of talking volume down (May 2026: "too early to expect rapid growth" before +9%) means the verbal framing sits below the numbers. The one way revenue misses by more than 3% is a data-centre phasing slip, which moves revenue between years rather than removing it.

Why the margin cone is moderate rather than wide. Three structural facts hold it in: about 90% of the cost base is variable, so a volume shortfall does not deleverage the margin the way it did at Bilfinger (less than 3% lower revenue, 100bp lower margin, because it kept its own skilled labour); half of revenue is service with indexation and three-to-seven-year contracts; and the largest contract is cost-plus. What widens it is that two of the three swing variables (D-03) are invisible until reported, and one (a project write-down) is binary.

Why the cash cone is wide. The advances are undisclosed in size and schedule; the pattern is known (advances in, then consumed over 18 to 24 months, then restored), the amounts are not. The model's FY2027 trough (free cash flow 51% of net income) and consensus's SEK 2.5bn of operating cash flow cannot both be right, and the difference is about a year of buyback capacity.

The skew, in probabilities (my judgement, built on the second set's 20/60/20).

  • Downside (about 20%): one problem contract of about SEK 250m, most likely at atNorth; core organic growth of about 2% as the Riksbank's November 2026 and February 2027 hikes bite earlier than modelled; drop-through about 6%. EPS about SEK 7.0. This case also removes most of the FY2027 cash and the third buyback.
  • Base (about 60%): as modelled. EPS about SEK 8.0 to 8.1.
  • Upside (about 20%): data-centre margin 7.5% or better, drop-through held near 14%, settlements near 3%, a further award without slippage. EPS about SEK 9.0. This is roughly DNB Carnegie's base case; consensus needs most of it.

Two things are asymmetric inside those cases. The time profile: the upside shows up early (Q3 and Q4 2026 volume, Stavanger cash, a third buyback, a dividend above consensus), the downside shows up late (margin from Q1 2027, cash from Q2 2027, a write-down whenever a project review finds it). And the magnitude: the largest single upside item (a SEK 1bn special distribution, feasible at 1.0 times EBITDA including leases) is worth about 3% of EPS, while the largest single downside item (a SEK 400m write-down) is worth about 17% of a year's EPS.

What the Q3 2026 report (tomorrow) does to the cone. It narrows the revenue cone and tells little about the margin cone. The second set's four tests stand: core margin up more than about 40bp year on year; Finland at or above 3.5%; the Swedish margin up by more than the SEK 16m-a-quarter saving explains; an order book above SEK 22bn with core intake still growing. Passing all four would justify raising the FY2027 margin to about 6.7% and closing half the gap to consensus; a Finnish or Swedish disappointment would justify 6.3%. A Q3 that shows the Stavanger cash and new advances in contract liabilities will make the cash picture look better than the FY2027 reality, which is the moment the Consumer should be most careful about extrapolation.

Peers in the same phase. The dispersion of installer outcomes two years into an upturn is large and sits mostly on the margin: Instalco went from about 8% (2021) to 5.8% (2025); Fasadgruppen's Q1 2026 adjusted EBITA fell to SEK 5m on weather; Bilfinger's 2026 guidance was cut from 6.0% to 3.2% to 3.6% in one announcement. Bravida's own 2023 (volume beat, then margin miss) is the median precedent. None of those peers had Bravida's service share or cost variability, which is why Bravida's cone is narrower than the sector's and why its 2023 miss was 30bp rather than 300bp.

Predictability, summarised. Revenue to end-2027: high. Adjusted EBITA margin to 6.6%: medium; above 6.6%: low. Cash in FY2027: low. Share count, interest, tax: high. The case is predictable enough to hold a view on the direction of estimate changes (down against consensus from mid-2027) and not predictable enough to hold a view on the exact year the 7% target is met, because the company has never met it and the evidence that it will is a broker's assumption on an undisclosed margin.

D-05

How helpful is the company's disclosure on operating momentum and key drivers of the thesis to monitor the case over about the next six months?

C55

Judgement. Disclosure is good enough to monitor the core recovery and the cash cycle precisely, and almost useless for monitoring the one driver that most separates the model from consensus. Within six months there are two reports (Q3 2026 on 23 October 2026; Q4 2026 and the full year on 10 February 2027, with the dividend proposal). Between them the Consumer can see every element of the model's core and cash paths, and nothing of the data-centre contract margin except through blunt proxies.

What is disclosed each quarter, and what in the model it tests.

Disclosure Tests Model marker to read it against
Organic growth by country; order intake and backlog by country Core volume; the data-centre ramp; the refill Q3 revenue +8.4% (organic about +10%); Q4 +10.6%; backlog above SEK 22bn after atNorth and EcoDataCenter are booked
Installation and service split by country Mix dilution Installation share rising from 52% while the data-centre work runs
Segment EBITA margin by country Sweden (savings; price), Denmark (hold at 5% plus), Norway (Green Mountain dilution from Q4), Finland (the atNorth tell) Sweden 7.7% for the year; Denmark 5.4%; Finland about 3.3% for FY2026 and above 3.5% in Q3
Items affecting comparability, bridged The clean base; the last restructuring charge (SEK 10 to 20m in Q3); any Stavanger interest booking Adjusted EBITA Q3 about SEK 420m (6.0%); Q4 about SEK 725m (8.3%)
Twelve-month cash conversion (company definition: operating cash flow plus tax and net interest paid less capital spending, over EBITDA) The cash cycle Back above 100% at Q4 2026 if the Stavanger principal and the advances land
Contract assets and contract liabilities, trade receivables and payables, in the notes The advances directly: liabilities rising in H2 2026 means prepayments received; falling through 2027 means consumption; contract assets rising faster than liabilities means milestones not being approved Contract liabilities above the Q2 2026 SEK 3,324m at Q4 2026; trade receivables down by about SEK 320m when Stavanger is paid
Net debt to EBITDA, company basis Distribution capacity About 0.8x including leases at year-end
Disputes, in the notes Stavanger receipt; the Danish hospital arbitration Cash in; no provision
LTIFR by country; headcount Capacity strain; the LTIP 2026 safety condition (injury rate below 4.5) Headcount rising again from about 13,400 would mean slack is being used

What is not disclosed, and why it matters.

  • No margin, milestone, prepayment or indexation terms on any of the four data-centre contracts, and no segment for the Special Projects office. The only read on the data-centre margin in six months is the Finnish segment margin with atNorth inside it from Q4 and the Norwegian margin with Green Mountain inside it from Q4, each diluted by the core. A Finnish margin of 4% in Q4 2026 could mean atNorth at 6% and the core at 3%, or atNorth at 3% and the core at 4.5%; the report will not say which.
  • No guidance and no building blocks to the 7% target, so there is no company statement to measure a quarter against; every "beat" or "miss" is against a consensus that is itself on mixed bases until February 2027 (DNB Carnegie excludes the ABEKA gain and the restructuring; Deutsche Bank includes the restructuring; the July estimates from Nordea and Oddo BHF probably include the gain).
  • Percentage-of-completion accounting means a project problem appears only when management's own forecast of the project changes; the quarter before a write-down looks normal. This is not a disclosure failing but it caps how early any problem can be seen.
  • The backlog is not split by contract type, so "core intake still growing" has to be inferred by subtracting the known awards from the total.

Two things management says that help. The chief executive frames volume cautiously, so his tone is a floor; and management does give specific, dated figures when it has them (restructuring charges by quarter, SEK 65m of savings, "at or around 5%" for Denmark). The absence of such a figure for the data-centre margin is therefore itself information: either it is not yet known internally at contract level, or it is not one management wants compared with 10%.

For the next six months, the monitoring list in priority order: (1) Finnish segment margin; (2) Swedish segment margin against the SEK 16m-a-quarter saving; (3) contract liabilities and trade receivables; (4) core order intake after subtracting known awards; (5) any new award and its structure (joint venture, package size), which tests the capacity ceiling; (6) the dividend proposal and any third programme in February. Items (1) to (4) are fully visible; (5) and (6) are events.

D-06

How clearly does what the company says it will achieve trace into specific operational drivers, onto identifiable revenue, cost and margin lines, and through to earnings per share; and how clear is the external transmission from value-chain dynamics to earnings per share?

C50

Question asked How clearly does what the company says it will achieve trace into specific operational drivers, onto identifiable revenue, cost and margin lines (the key fundamental milestones and measures), and through to earnings per share; and how clear is the external transmission from value-chain dynamics to earnings per share?

Judgement. The internal transmission is clear for everything that is in the model's base case and opaque for the one thing that is not: what a principal-contractor data-centre job earns. The external transmission is unusually clear for an industrial company, because the lags are documented and the cost base is variable; what is unclear is not the mechanism but the timing of one input (the spring 2027 settlements) and the identity of one (the next data-centre award).

What the company says it will achieve. Five undated targets (growth above 5%, EBITA margin above 7%, cash conversion above 100%, net debt below 2.5 times EBITDA, payout at least 50%); SEK 65m of Swedish savings from 2026 with SEK 90 to 100m of charges; "margin over volume"; growth "as market conditions permit"; and continuous evaluation of buybacks to the 2027 AGM. Only the savings and the charges are building blocks. The 7% target has no bridge, and the Capital Markets Day did not supply one.

The internal trace, item by item (FY2027, on the model's lines; EPS effects on about 202m shares at 21% tax).

Driver Line it lands on Size EPS Clarity
Core volume: organic +4.5% (2 points volume, 2.5 points price) on about SEK 29bn Revenue, then EBITA at a fading incremental margin (about 12% on volume, the average margin on price) about SEK 110m of extra EBITA about +0.43 Clear; the drop-through is the only judgement
Swedish savings Swedish segment EBITA SEK 65m run-rate, SEK 25m incremental in FY2027 +0.10 incremental Clear, dated
Finland from about 3.3% to about 4.0% to 4.3% Finnish segment EBITA about SEK 24m incremental +0.09 Clear in arithmetic; depends on atNorth (preface)
Denmark 5.4% to 5.7%; Norway core flat, diluted by Green Mountain Segment EBITA about SEK 30m net +0.12 Clear
Data-centre contracts: SEK 3.3bn at 5.25% net of SEK 25m allowance Revenue in Norway, Finland and Sweden; EBITA in those segments; no separate line about SEK 173m (8% of EBITA) +0.67 Opaque: no contract margin disclosed; cost-plus on the largest piece known only through DNB Carnegie
2027 wage lag Gross margin on the Swedish fixed-price backlog (about SEK 3 to 4bn executed in 2027) −SEK 25m central; −60 to −80 tail −0.10; −0.25 to −0.30 Mechanism clear (2023 precedent); size unknown until spring 2027
Net financial items SEK −120m (lease interest about 55; bank interest the rest; SEK 15m per point of rate) Clear; consensus's SEK 60 to 67m is the one line where consensus is mechanically wrong
Tax 21%; shares −1% a year +0.08 from the count Clear
Advances consumed Working capital, not earnings: about SEK 860m outflow none Mechanism clear; amount undisclosed

Read down the table: about SEK 0.9 of the FY2027 EPS step over FY2026 traces to lines the company reports and to programmes it has named; about SEK 0.7 (the data-centre contribution) traces to segment lines in which it is invisible. The model's base case is therefore mostly auditable from the outside, and the part that is not is also the part where the model and consensus disagree most.

The external trace.

  1. Construction starts to installation revenue. Swedish building starts +8.0% in July 2026 (non-residential +13.2%) reach installation revenue two to four quarters later; Veidekke's +9% production in 2026 and +4% in 2027 is the market path. Clear and well-documented; this is the sector's own stated pattern ("revenue lags construction starts by several quarters").
  2. Policy rates to starts. The Riksbank's signalled hikes (to 2.00% in November 2026 and 2.25% by February 2027, Nordea) and Norges Bank at 4.50% act on 2027 starts and so on 2028 installation revenue. Clear in direction, with the same lag. This is why the model's FY2028 to FY2029 core slows and why the risk to the next 18 months from rates is small.
  3. Wage agreements to margin. The spring 2027 "mark" from manufacturing sets the installation agreements; Bravida prices expected increases into tenders; Danish jobs over 12 months are indexed; Swedish fixed-price work is not. The transmission is one number (settlements less what was priced) times one exposure (the unindexed Swedish backlog). Clear mechanism, unknown input until about April 2027.
  4. Copper and equipment to the data-centre contracts. Green Mountain passes cost through; atNorth's terms are undisclosed; equipment is largely owner-furnished in hyperscale work (70% to 80% globally), which removes procurement risk and procurement margin together. Clear for the largest contract, unclear for the second.
  5. Equipment lead times to revenue timing. Transformer lead times move revenue between quarters and years; they do not remove it. Clear.
  6. The AI financing cycle to the book. It acts on the next awards (the refill from 2028), not on the contracted three; atNorth's and EcoDataCenter's funding is now evidenced, Green Mountain's Stavanger financing is not. Partially clear.
  7. Competitors' capacity to core price. The National Institute of Economic Research's "significantly improved pricing environment" (DNB Carnegie, 28 September 2026) is the balancing loop working in Bravida's favour; it reverses with a two-to-four-quarter lag if the 2028 recovery stalls. Clear mechanism, with the trigger outside the 18-month window.
  8. Assemblin Caverion's data-centre business area (31 August 2026) to the next award. Clear in direction (two bidders instead of one), unobservable in size until a tender is lost.

Where the trace breaks. Three places: the contract margin on the data-centre book (internal); the milestone and prepayment schedule (internal, cash); and the 7% target itself, which has no bridge from the company and whose brokers' bridges rest on a 10% data-centre margin and a 7.0% core that the company has never reported. The memo can state the model's EPS path as a chain of named, reported lines; it cannot state the company's own path, because the company has not given one.

D-07

To what extent do the share price and the multiple move with sector and industry peers rather than idiosyncratically (on a rolling six-month basis), and so how far is the multiple company-driven rather than industry-driven?

❌ D38

Judgement. The share price has become almost entirely company-driven, and so has the multiple. Over the latest six months the dashboard's peer basket explains 10% of Bravida's daily price variance (correlation 0.32, beta 0.65); 90% is idiosyncratic. Bravida returned +53% in that window while the equal-weighted basket returned −3.5%. The multiple tells the same story: Bravida's 24-month-forward P/E is 16.0x to 16.5x (76th percentile of five years, 95th of two), while Instalco, the one peer on the same Swedish recovery with the same sell-side momentum rating (A), is at 12.9x (its own 46th percentile) and SPIE, the peer with a data-centre and grid narrative, is at 11.7x. The premium of about three and a half turns over Instalco is the market's price for the data-centre story and the self-help story; it was earned by Bravida alone and it will be kept or lost on Bravida's own prints. For the predictability of the forecasts that is neutral; for the predictability of the share price it is negative, because an idiosyncratic multiple at the 95th percentile of two years has only one source of support.

The co-movement record.

  • Rolling six-month R-squared against the basket: 35% at November 2024, 24% to 33% through mid-2025, then a step down to 6% to 9% from October 2025 (when the shares were at their SEK 75 low and the recovery story began), a brief rise to 26% at April 2026 (the sector-wide rally into the spring), and back to 10% to 17% from May 2026 as the data-centre awards arrived.
  • The highest pairwise correlation is with Instalco (0.34, R-squared 12%); every other peer is below 0.26, and Arcadis and Bodycote are nil. The basket is heterogeneous (equipment rental, consulting, heat treatment, Swiss and German installers), so part of the low figure is the basket, but the Instalco pair is the fair test and it also says mostly idiosyncratic.
  • Beta to the basket of 0.65 with a +53% return means the return was not a leveraged sector move; it was a re-rating plus estimate upgrades specific to the company (FY2027 consensus EPS +15.9% in six months; target prices +32%).

What drove the idiosyncratic part. In order: the three data-centre awards (June to August 2026; "let's get structural", DNB Carnegie, 1 July); the Q2 2026 beat (organic +9% against about +2%); the Stavanger ruling (8 June); the buybacks; and the Q4 2025 turn. Each is company-specific, and each is now in the price. The decelerating momentum (3-month +9.6% against 6-month +51.8%; the price at SEK 144.4 on 21 October against SEK 149 on 16 October and a 52-week high of SEK 152.8) says the idiosyncratic drivers have been absorbed and the share is waiting for the next one.

What the multiple is, and is not, tied to.

  • Not to the sector's multiple: the installer peers de-rated or stood still while Bravida re-rated (Bilfinger from about 12x to 6.5x forward EV/EBIT after its warning; SPIE flat; Instalco flat at its median).
  • Not to the discount rate: the first set's point (a US 10-year near 5.1% in a regime refusing re-ratings) applies to all peers equally and did not stop Bravida's re-rating.
  • To its own delivery: DNB Carnegie's bear case (1% organic growth, 6.0% margin) carries a 10x EV/EBITA multiple; Deutsche Bank's trough reference is 11.3x EV/EBITA; the first set's trough ladder gives SEK 105 to 110 on unchanged earnings at the five-year 25th percentile P/E and SEK 90 to 98 with a 10% earnings cut. Those are company-specific de-rating paths, not sector ones.

Two consequences for the memo.

  1. Peer read-across is weak as a hedge and strong as a signal. A good Instalco Q3 (expected adjusted EBITA +33% year on year, DNB Carnegie) will not carry Bravida's price; a bad one would read across to the Swedish core, which is 45% of the model's EPS gain. The asymmetry is unhelpful.
  2. The multiple's level is for the memo's fair-multiple topic, not for this question, but one fact belongs here. The model's guideline fair multiple (19.38x at the 18-month column) sits at the ten-year 90th percentile band, and the first set's own view (C-50, C-83) was that the profile merits a multiple near the ten-year median of 14.6x. With 90% of price variance idiosyncratic, the choice between those two is the largest single determinant of the return, and it rests on the same undisclosed margin as the earnings gap. The Consumer should treat the multiple as a second, correlated bet on data-centre delivery, not as an independent cushion.

In one line. The share and the multiple are company-driven, the company-driven story is fully in the price, and the next company-specific news on the margin (not on volume) decides whether the premium holds.

Back to contents

Layer 4: Assessing the extent to which our assessment of the foundational components and financial forecasts is reflected or not in current company guidance, sell-side estimates and stock market narrative, primarily for the next 18 months and secondarily for 18 months to 3 years out

The Investment Analyst's heading The fourth set: the extent to which our assessment is reflected in guidance, sell-side estimates and the stock market narrative

The fourth set as a whole ❌ D37 written last by the Investment Analyst; shown first here

Judgement. Guidance, estimates and narrative reflect our assessment of the volume recovery fully, our assessment of the capital returns fully, and our assessment of the margin and the cash hardly at all.

  • Guidance (I-01): there is none to move, the undated margin target sits 0.5pp above our path for the whole period and consensus has adopted it as its FY2027 to FY2028 forecast, and the company will not supply a bridge. The one thing the company can do that we expect and the market half-expects is distribute more; it is worth about 3% of EPS per SEK 1bn.
  • Estimates (I-02): above us by 8% to 10% on FY2027 to FY2028 EPS and by about 70% on FY2027 operating cash flow; rising for two more prints on volume and a stale dividend line, then falling from the first half of 2027 as the margin and the cash become visible. Breadth is cresting; the stock is at its targets.
  • Narrative (I-03): complete on the upside, led by one house, with the three deciding dynamics either unexamined or carried at a value (a 10% data-centre margin) that the structure of the largest contract argues against. Investor commentary is unread and should be commissioned.

The steer for the memo's topics #4, #7 and #8. There is no conservatism in guidance to lean on, no estimate momentum left to ride beyond February 2027, and a narrative that would need to discover three negatives before it discovers any positive we hold. The positive we hold (a predictable, well-founded revenue recovery) is already the consensus view. The memo's honest framing is that the gap between our assessment and the market is adverse on the next 18 months, that it closes from mid-2027 through estimate cuts of roughly 8% and a multiple that is 90% company-driven, and that the case becomes interesting again at the first set's trough levels or on evidence, from the Finnish and Norwegian segment margins, that the data-centre contracts earn what DNB Carnegie assumes. The next two prints will look good and will not be that evidence.

Guidance

1 question
I-0140
I-01

How likely is company guidance to be raised, met or reduced at the next few earnings events (all such events over the next 18 months), and what would drive each outcome? Secondarily, how likely is any guidance, mid-term target or ambition for 18 months to three years out to be raised, met or reduced?

❌ D40

Judgement. Bravida has no guidance to raise, meet or reduce, and the Capital Markets Day of 27 May 2026 showed that it will not acquire any: it reconfirmed the five undated targets and gave no figures. So the question becomes two others. First, at each of the six reports in the next 18 months, which of the five targets will the rolling twelve-month figures meet, and will management say anything that functions as guidance? Second, is there any realistic probability that the targets are dated, raised, or replaced? My answers: growth, leverage and payout will be met at every event; cash conversion will be met at the two year-end events and missed through 2027; the margin target will be met at none of the six events in the model's case and at the last two in consensus's; management will keep talking volume down and margin not at all; and the probability of any change to the targets in 18 months is low, of a raise very low.

The events and what each will show, in the model's case.

Event Date Rolling twelve-month figures on the model's path Targets met What management is likely to say
Q3 2026 23 October 2026 Growth about 4%; adjusted margin about 6.3%; cash conversion rising from 77% if Stavanger and the first advances land; leverage about 1.0x Leverage, payout Order book, data-centre start, "gradual" Swedish improvement; the last restructuring charge; possibly a third buyback
Q4 2026 and FY2026 10 February 2027 Growth +7.8%; margin 6.4%; cash conversion above 100% on the Stavanger cash and advances; leverage about 0.8x Growth, cash, leverage, payout (dividend proposal about SEK 4.20 against consensus 4.00) A record order book; possibly an extra distribution or larger programme; no margin timeline
AGM late April 2027 Buyback mandate renewed
Q1 2027 early May 2027 Growth about 10%; margin about 6.45%; cash conversion falling as advances are consumed Growth, leverage, payout First full data-centre quarter; harder core comparisons; "too early" language on margin
Q2 2027 mid July 2027 Growth about 11%; margin about 6.5%; cash conversion below 100% Growth, leverage, payout The wage round, known by then; atNorth inside the Finnish margin
Q3 2027 late October 2027 Growth about 11%; margin about 6.5%; cash conversion at its low Growth, leverage, payout Working capital explained as milestone timing
Q4 2027 and FY2027 February 2028 Growth +11.6%; margin 6.4%; cash conversion about 51% on free cash flow over net income (higher on the company's measure) Growth, leverage, payout The FY2028 outlook; the refill question

On consensus's path the margin target is met at the FY2027 report (7.0%) and cash conversion above 100% at every year-end. The disagreement between the model and consensus is therefore visible as a disagreement about whether the company meets its own margin target in February 2028, and the company will not arbitrate it.

What would make management speak. Three things have made it give figures before: a programme with a cost (the Swedish merger), a turnaround with a milestone (Denmark "at or around 5%") and a capital decision (the buybacks). The equivalents in the next 18 months are a third or larger buyback (likely at or after the February 2027 report, given the Stavanger cash, about SEK 1.5bn of bank-debt headroom below 1.0 times EBITDA excluding leases, and Deutsche Bank's public criticism of the first programme as "formulaic"), a dividend above consensus (likely), and, less likely, a stated Finnish margin ambition once atNorth is running. A margin bridge to 7% is the one thing I would not expect; the company has never given one and the Capital Markets Day was the occasion to do so.

What would reduce the informal message. A project review at atNorth or Green Mountain that changes the forecast of a contract (a write-down appears in the quarter it is found, with no warning); a Finnish margin back below 3%; or a Swedish settlement well above 3%. Each would come with the chief executive's usual framing, which has been accurate on the specific and silent on the general.

The 18-month to three-year horizon. The targets are undated and have survived ten years of the margin target being missed; they will survive FY2027. Probability that the margin target is met in FY2028 (the first year within the three-year window): about 30% (the model has 6.55%; consensus 7.1%). Probability that the board dates the targets or sets a new set at a future Capital Markets Day before end-2028: perhaps 30%, and if it did, the realistic new margin figure would be 7% dated, not a higher one, because the LTIP hurdles it sets internally (FY2026 at SEK 2,144m, about 10% above the model) show a board that sets stretch levels and accepts partial vesting (LTIP 2022 paid 31.9%). Probability of a raise to any target: under 10%. The growth target will look conservative against FY2026 to FY2027 and then correct against FY2028 to FY2029 as the data-centre book completes; that sequence is the model's, and consensus's single FY2029 estimate (revenue −4.8%) agrees with it.

Why the score is below the midpoint. The question's score measures how favourable the gap is between our assessment and what guidance reflects. Growth: the target is below our path, favourable but already obvious. Margin: the target is above our path by 0.5pp for the whole period, unfavourable, and it is the figure consensus has adopted. Cash: the target is above our FY2027 path. There is no guidance to raise and so no raise catalyst; the capital-return intent is the one place the company can surprise, and it is small in EPS terms (3% for SEK 1bn).
