Sell-side estimates

1 question
I-0238
I-02

How likely are sell-side consensus forecasts, and the net breadth of revisions, to move higher or lower over the next few earnings events (over the next 18 months): to be upgraded, cut or held, and why? Secondarily, how likely are the consensus forecasts for 18 months to three years out to be upgraded, cut or held?

❌ D38

Judgement. Consensus will most probably rise a little over the next two reports and fall by more over the following four, ending the 18 months below where it is today on FY2027 and FY2028 earnings and well below on FY2027 cash. The net breadth of revisions, now positive but cresting (sell-side momentum rated A on the dashboard; FY2027 EPS +1.8% over one month, +9.0% over three, +15.9% over six; buy share down 20 points to 60% over six months; targets at the price), turns negative in the first half of 2027. My central path for FY2027 consensus EPS (median SEK 8.78; mean 8.59) is about SEK 8.9 by February 2027 and about SEK 8.2 to 8.5 by the end of 2027; the probability that it is higher in twelve months than today is about 25%, held within 3% about 25%, lower by more than 3% about 50%.

Where consensus stands, and its construction. Six brokers; three refreshed since September (the restricted broker, 12 October, EPS 7.10, 9.00, 9.30, Hold, target SEK 156; DNB Carnegie, 2 October, 6.96, 8.70, 9.47, Buy, SEK 173; Pareto, 28 September, 7.13, 8.57, 9.44, Hold, SEK 145), one bearish outlier (Deutsche Bank, 7 September, 6.50, 7.40, 7.80, Hold, SEK 106, organic growth 1.9% in 2026 and 4.1% in 2027, which the Q2 2026 print has already overtaken) and two stale July estimates (Nordea 7.27, 8.76, 9.93; Oddo BHF 7.46, 8.38, 9.46, both dated 13 July, before the SEK 850m award and before the Stavanger finality). Targets span SEK 106 to 173; the mean is 152 against a price of 144 to 149. The three current estimates for FY2027 cluster at 8.57 to 9.00, so the "consensus" the memo should compare the model with is about SEK 8.7, not 8.78.

The record of revisions after prints, which sets the pattern. Q3 2025: a 5% EBITA miss on Swedish pricing and Finnish write-downs; cuts. Q4 2025: a beat, +3% to 4% to FY2026 to FY2027 EBITA. Q1 2026: a revenue beat; DNB Carnegie raised FY2026 EPS 7%, half of it the ABEKA gain. Q2 2026: a clear beat (adjusted EBITA +11% against consensus; organic +9% against about +2%); FY2027 EPS +9% over the following three months. The pattern is the first set's: beats on volume in the upswing, misses on project margin later. The cumulative revision from the February 2026 low (FY2027 mean SEK 7.35) to now (8.59) is +17%, against a share price up about 90% from its low; the market has re-rated the stock by more than the estimates moved.

Event by event.

  1. Q3 2026 (23 October). Likely a volume beat against easy comparisons (Q3 2025 organic −2.5%, adjusted EBITA SEK 342m; the model has SEK 420m, +23%), the Stavanger cash and new advances, and an order book above SEK 22bn. FY2026 EPS held or +1% to 2%; FY2027 +0% to 3% as the two stale brokers refresh toward the three current ones. Risk of a cut: low (15%), and only if the Finnish or Swedish margin disappoints.
  2. Q4 2026 (10 February 2027). Likely strong on volume (organic about +13%), cash conversion back above 100%, a dividend above the stale consensus of SEK 4.00 (DNB Carnegie SEK 4.42; the model 4.20), possibly a larger programme. Dividend consensus up; FY2027 EPS held to +2%. This is the high point of the revision cycle. Reported FY2026 EPS (about SEK 7.6 to 8.1) will print far above the adjusted consensus of 7.20, and some commentary will call it a beat; it is definitional.
  3. Q1 2027 (May). The first quarter with the full data-centre run-rate and harder core comparisons (Q1 2026 organic +0.9% was the easy one; from Q2 2027 the comparison is +9%). If the core drop-through fades as the model expects, the FY2027 margin of 7.0% starts to look stretched. FY2027 EPS −2% to −4%; the buy share slips. If instead the Swedish margin shows real price, held.
  4. Q2 2027 (July). The settlements are known; atNorth is visible inside the Finnish margin for two quarters. Either the margin thesis is confirmed (upside case, held) or FY2027 and FY2028 are cut a further 3% to 5%.
  5. Q3 2027 (October). The cash trough: operating cash flow tracking about SEK 1.5bn against consensus 2.5bn. Cash and free cash flow estimates cut; EPS cut 1% to 3% on net interest and the smaller buyback.
  6. Q4 2027 (February 2028). FY2027 actual EPS about 8.0 to 8.1 against today's 8.78: a 7% to 9% shortfall unless the upside case has played. FY2028 consensus (9.45; the model 8.57) cut toward 8.6 to 8.9; FY2029 (one broker, 8.80) held or cut depending on the refill.

What would produce upgrades instead. (a) Data-centre margins visible at 8% to 10% through the Finnish and Norwegian segments from Q4 2026, which would validate DNB Carnegie's base case and lift FY2027 EPS toward SEK 9.0; (b) core Swedish margins up by more than the savings explain, which would signal real price; (c) a new award of SEK 2bn or more in H1 2027 structured as a joint venture or package the central office can run in parallel, which would refill FY2028 to FY2029 and lift the single FY2029 estimate; (d) a SEK 1bn distribution. I put the combined probability that consensus FY2027 EPS is above SEK 9.0 in twelve months at about 20%, matching the upside case.

What would produce cuts larger than the model implies. A write-down of SEK 250m or more (EPS −1.0 in the year; FY2027 consensus −10% to −12% in one step); a Swedish settlement above 4% with visible pass-through failure; a stalled core order intake in H1 2027 (the first set's warning indicator: core organic growth below 3% for two quarters). Combined probability about 20%.

Breadth. With six brokers, breadth moves in steps of 17 points. The two stale July estimates will refresh at the Q3 print and will probably move toward the current three, which is a small upgrade in the mean but not in the median. Deutsche Bank at SEK 106 is the one obvious candidate to move up (its organic growth forecast is already wrong), which would lift the mean target and the mean EPS without changing the median; it is a Hold and would stay one. The buy share is more likely to fall than rise over the 18 months as targets (mean SEK 152) are reached and the margin evidence arrives; a reading of 50% (three of six) by mid-2027 is the central case.

The 18-month to three-year horizon. FY2028 consensus (EPS 9.45, EBIT margin 7.1%, revenue 35.1bn with DNB Carnegie at 36.3bn) is more exposed than FY2027: it assumes the margin step is kept while the data-centre book completes and the Swedish wage reset is absorbed, and it assumes revenue growth of 3.6% in a year the model has at 2.4% and the cycle turning. The single FY2029 estimate (revenue −4.8%, EPS 8.80) is the brokers' own admission of the roll-off; it coincides with the model's FY2029 EPS by a different route (higher revenue, lower margin). Direction for FY2028: down by 7% to 9% over 18 months in the central case; for FY2029: held, with the refill as the swing. A raise of the out-years needs the refill, and the refill needs the capacity ceiling to be loosened; neither is visible yet.

Why the score is where it is. Near term the gap is mildly favourable (two prints of likely volume beats and a dividend above a stale consensus); over 18 months it is unfavourable (FY2027 to FY2028 EPS about 8% to 10% above the model, cash about 70% above, and the direction of breadth turning). The dashboard's A for sell-side momentum describes the past six months, not the next six.

Stock market narrative

1 question
I-0335
I-03

How comprehensively are these dynamics articulated in the current narrative of (i) sell-side commentary and (ii) investor commentary: how well known is each of them to investors who own or cover the company or its sector, primarily for the next 18 months and secondarily for 18 months to three years out?

❌ D35

Question asked How comprehensively are these dynamics articulated in the current narrative of (i) sell-side commentary and (ii) investor commentary (for example on Twitter): how well known is each of them to investors who own or cover the company or its sector, primarily for the next 18 months and secondarily for 18 months to three years out?

Judgement. The narrative is comprehensive on the opportunity and selective on the economics. Every dynamic that lifts the case is articulated, prominently, and mostly by one house (DNB Carnegie, whose "Let's get structural" of 1 July 2026 and "The watts and whys of the data centre opportunity" of 8 September 2026 set the frame); every dynamic that caps it is either acknowledged in words and absent from the numbers, or absent altogether. The three dynamics that decide FY2027 to FY2028 earnings against consensus (the fade of the core incremental margin, the data-centre contract margin, and the FY2027 working-capital reversal) are, respectively, discussed by no one, assumed at 10% by the house that leads the narrative, and warned about in prose by that same house while its cash forecast ignores the warning. Investor commentary on X and Swedish forums could not be accessed by the commissioned report, which is itself a gap the memo should state; the dashboard's indirect reads (interest level A, first decile; relative strength at the 95th percentile of the universe; 3-month return +9.6% after 6-month +51.8%) describe a well-followed, well-owned stock whose momentum is decelerating.

Coverage caveat. AlphaSense holds research from six houses (DNB Carnegie, Deutsche Bank, Pareto, Nordea, Oddo BHF, ABG Sundal Collier); the commissioned narrative report could cite only DNB Carnegie's and Deutsche Bank's own documents, so it covers two of six (33%, below the 80% the brief required). Pareto (Hold, SEK 145, 28 September), Nordea (Buy, SEK 149, July), Oddo BHF (Buy, SEK 150, July) and the restricted broker (Hold, SEK 156, 12 October) are known only through their FactSet figures. What follows credits a view to a house only where its own document is cited; the "unknown" column is therefore an upper bound on how little is known.

Dynamic by dynamic.

Danish arbitration (2028) DNB Carnegie (28 September): write-down risk; the annual report; no provision A key risk A binary outside the base case; the remaining exposure is below SEK 800m after the SEK 100m settlement in Q4 2025 Known; the smaller size is not

LTIP hurdle (SEK 2,144m on FY2026) DNB Carnegie notes its FY2026 EBITA of SEK 2,028m is below it A stretch Agreed; no planning signal Known, correctly read by DNB Carnegie

Peers' narratives, and what they say about Bravida's.

  • Instalco (+64.5% over twelve months, buy share 86%, sell-side momentum A, P/E 12.9x at its own 46th percentile): the same Swedish recovery, the same restructuring-and-pricing story, a stated 8% target, and no data-centre story. The market has paid Instalco nothing for its recovery multiple and Bravida about three and a half turns for the data-centre story. That is the cleanest measure of how much of Bravida's narrative is the structural claim.
  • SPIE (P/E 11.7x, flat multiple): a data-centre and grid narrative with 70% service revenue and a 7.6% margin, and no re-rating. European investors have not paid for data-centre exposure as such; they have paid Bravida for a step-change in growth. If the growth step ends in 2028 (the model), the SPIE precedent says the premium goes.
  • Comfort Systems (34x P/E, 93% installation, in-house prefabrication): the template DNB Carnegie's bulls cite; DNB Carnegie itself notes Bravida lacks the prefabrication capability. The comparison is known and its weakness is known to the house that makes it.
  • Bilfinger (−38% over twelve months; forward EV/EBIT from about 12x to 6.5x after the September 2026 warning): the sector's live precedent that a technical-services re-rating reverses in a single print. Known to the sector; not applied to Bravida in any document in evidence.
  • Fasadgruppen (−56%) and Burkhalter (−8.7%): the cohort's laggards, on leverage and on flat growth respectively; they frame the dispersion of outcomes rather than Bravida's narrative.

For 18 months to three years. The narrative's out-year content is DNB Carnegie's 2030 scenario (data centres at 20% to 30% of EBITA; EBITA SEK 3.6bn and a share price toward SEK 275 in the bull case). Nothing in the narrative addresses the FY2028 to FY2029 plateau as a consequence of the capacity ceiling; the single FY2029 revenue estimate (−4.8%) shows one broker has modelled the roll-off without naming it. The out-year narrative is therefore more detached from our assessment than the 18-month one.

Why the score is low. The score measures how favourable the gap is between our assessment and what the narrative reflects. The dynamics the narrative does not carry are the negative ones (margin fade, a contract margin half DNB Carnegie's, a cash trough, a structural plateau); the dynamics it carries fully are the positive ones (the awards, Stavanger, distributions, the structural claim). The only favourable gaps are small: the dividend above a stale consensus, the near-term volume beats against the chief executive's framing, and the clean (unfactored) cash number. A narrative that knows the upside and not the downside is the least favourable configuration for an incoming holder and the most favourable for one considering the trough levels in the first set's C-83.

Appendix Research requests the Investment Analyst raised for the memo

Research requests for the next set

Company: Bravida Holding AB (BRAV-SE, Nasdaq Stockholm): a Nordic multi-technical installation and service contractor (electrical, heating, plumbing, ventilation, security, building automation) reporting four country segments (Sweden, Denmark, Norway, Finland), with sales split between project installation (about 52% in FY2025) and recurring service and maintenance (about 48%), now delivering large data-centre contracts for Green Mountain (Norway, through the DC Nordic joint venture with Backe Industri), atNorth (Finland, as principal contractor) and EcoDataCenter (Sweden).

Request: OWN-1

Question: What did Bravida Holding AB's (BRAV-SE) Q3 2026 interim report of 23 October 2026, and Instalco's (INSTAL-SE) Q3 2026 report of the same week, show on organic growth by country, EBITA margin by country (Sweden, Denmark, Norway and Finland), order intake and order backlog, items affecting comparability (the Swedish restructuring charge and any booking of the New Stavanger University Hospital interest and cost award), twelve-month cash conversion, trade receivables, contract assets and contract liabilities, net debt, and any new share buyback programme or distribution; what did management say on the call about the pricing environment, the start of the Green Mountain and atNorth projects, prepayments received, the Finnish margin, and the 2027 Swedish wage round; and how did each covering broker (DNB Carnegie, Deutsche Bank, Pareto Securities, Nordea, Oddo BHF, ABG Sundal Collier and any other) change its FY2026 to FY2028 revenue, EBITA, EPS, dividend and operating cash flow estimates, rating and target price in the two weeks after the report?

Request: OWN-2

Question: For Bravida Holding AB (BRAV-SE), what EBITA margin do Nordic contractors earn on data-centre installation and principal-contractor work, with evidence from YIT's data-centre contracting (which YIT said on 24 September 2026 exceeds its 6% contracting EBIT margin target), from Caverion's and Assemblin Caverion's data-centre projects, and from any disclosed cost-plus or target-cost fee levels on Nordic data-centre contracts; what is Bravida's exact scope, contract form and liability at atNorth's FIN04 campus in Kouvola relative to YIT's EUR 300 million design-build contract (announced 21 July 2026), Peab's EUR 130 million building contract and NRC Group's EUR 25 million substation contract, including which party carries schedule and liquidated-damages risk; and what is the customer, location and scope of the data-centre installation contract of approximately SEK 850 million that Bravida announced on 25 August 2026, and is it separate from the SEK 650 million EcoDataCenter contract in Borlänge announced on 7 July 2026?

Request: OWN-3

Question: What do the research notes of Pareto Securities (Hold, target SEK 145, 28 September 2026), Nordea Equity Research (Buy, target SEK 149, July 2026), Oddo BHF (Buy, target SEK 150, July 2026), ABG Sundal Collier and any other house covering Bravida Holding AB (BRAV-SE) say about the EBITA margin they assume on the data-centre contracts, the margin they assume on the rest of the business in FY2027 to FY2028, FY2027 operating cash flow and the consumption of customer advances, the 2027 Swedish wage round, the Danish public-sector arbitration due in 2028, and the risk of an order-book gap when the Green Mountain, atNorth and EcoDataCenter projects complete in 2028 to 2029; and what do investors say about Bravida on X, on Swedish investor forums (for example Placera and the Avanza forum) and in Swedish financial media since June 2026, on the same topics and on the share's valuation after its rise, with dates and how prominently each topic features?

Terms used in the answers

Hover over a dotted term on a computer to see it written out. On a phone, this list is the reference. Terms not listed are company, product or trial names, or terms the Investment Analyst explains where it uses them.

AGM
annual general meeting
CFO
chief financial officer
D&A
depreciation and amortisation
DNB
DNB Carnegie, a Nordic broker
EBIT
earnings before interest and tax (operating profit)
EBITA
earnings before interest, tax and amortisation
EBITDA
earnings before interest, tax, depreciation and amortisation
EPS
earnings per share
EUR
euro
FCF
free cash flow
FX
foreign exchange (currency)
FY
fiscal year (FY2027 or FY27 is the company's fiscal year 2027; the company's year-end is in its reports)
GDP
gross domestic product
H1
first half of the fiscal year
H2
second half of the fiscal year
IFRS
International Financial Reporting Standards
M&A
mergers and acquisitions
Q1
first quarter
Q2
second quarter
Q3
third quarter
Q4
fourth quarter
SEK
Swedish krona
USD
United States dollar
